América Airports (CAAP) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Argentina's concession now runs to 2056 with new investment commitments.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 19, 2026 at 12:53PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Management of Corporación América Airports (CAAP -1.90%) reported revenue growth supported by resilient international travel demand and significant strategic developments. The company reached a major milestone by amending its Argentine concession agreement, which includes a multiyear term extension and the implementation of dollar-denominated tariffs. While domestic traffic in certain markets faced capacity constraints, the broader portfolio demonstrated strength through growth in international traffic. The company maintained a low leverage ratio and successfully refinanced debt post-quarter to support its multiyear investment program.

Operator: Good morning, and welcome to Corporación América Airports Third Quarter 2026 Conference Call. A slide presentation accompanies today's webcast and is available in the Investors section of the company's website. [Operator Instructions] At this time, I would like to turn the call over to Iñaki Esnaola, Head of Investor Relations. Please go ahead.

Patricio Esnaola: Thank you. Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us today. Speaking during today's call will be Martin Eurnekian, our Chief Executive Officer; and Jorge Arruda, our Chief Financial Officer. Before we proceed, I would like to make the following safe harbor statement. Today's call will contain forward-looking statements, and I refer you to the forward-looking statements section of our earnings release and recent filings with the SEC. We assume no obligation to update or revise any forward-looking statements to reflect new or changed events or circumstances. Please note that throughout this call, all references to revenues, costs, adjusted EBITDA and margin will refer to figures excluding IFRIC 12. Also, all comparisons discussed are year-over-year unless otherwise noted.

I will now turn the call over to our CEO, Martin Eurnekian.

Martin Francisco Eurnekian: Thank you, Iñaki, and good morning to everyone joining us today. The third quarter was marked by strong revenue growth, resilient international passenger demand and significant progress on the strategic front. Passenger traffic increased modestly with international travel continuing to grow across all markets. In Argentina, domestic traffic remained affected by airline capacity constraints, although we saw an encouraging recovery in September after several months of declines. Revenue growth was a clear highlight, increasing nearly 14%, well ahead of the low single-digit increase in passenger traffic. Both our aeronautical and commercial businesses contributed to this performance with further improvement in revenue per passenger, reflecting our continued focus on commercial execution.

On profitability, adjusted EBITDA remained broadly in line with last year despite continued pressure in Argentina from weaker domestic traffic, a challenging comparison base in our cargo business and higher peso-denominated operating costs, reflecting inflation outpacing currency depreciation. Importantly, all our other countries delivered EBITDA growth, reinforcing the strength of our portfolio. We maintained a very solid financial position with healthy liquidity and low leverage, providing significant flexibility to invest in our operations and pursue new growth opportunities. Beyond our operating performance, in September, we have achieved a major milestone with the amendment agreement entered into with the government of Argentina. Let me now turn to Slide 4 to discuss the main aspects of this agreement. First, starting with tariffs.

All passenger and aircraft fees are now denominated in U.S. dollars. The domestic passenger use fee will increase to $9 effective December 10, compared with an effective tariff of approximately $3.7 in the third quarter. In addition, all tariffs will be adjusted based on U.S. CPI. Adjustment to international and regional passenger fees will begin in January 2029, while adjustments to domestic passenger fees will begin in January 2030. In both cases, adjustments will be made annually. Aircraft fees will also be indexed to U.S. CPI with adjustments taking place every 3 years starting in 2029. Second, the agreement establishes a total investment program of approximately $7.3 billion over the remaining concession term.

Of this amount, $6.7 billion will be funded through the existing trust structure, while $600 million will be invested directly by Aeropuertos Argentina. The trust will continue to be funded by the existing concession fee of 15% of gross revenues. Third, certain maintenance expenses that are currently funded directly by Aeropuertos Argentina will now be funded by the trust upon completion of the $600 million investment program. Lastly, the agreement includes additional safeguards to preserve the concession economic and financial equilibrium. The regulatory IRR was set at 14.3%, while maintaining our right to seek further contractual adjustments. Financial projections will be reviewed every 3 years, starting in 2029 to assess whether additional adjustments are needed.

Furthermore, if investments beyond the agreed $600 million are done, the amendment provides mechanism to maintain the concession economic equilibrium, including the creation of a technical panel. This amendment represents a major milestone as it combines a material term extension, the increase of tariffs, the inclusion of tariff adjustments and strengthen the mechanisms designed to preserve the concessions economic equilibrium over time. The concession extension of 18 years of our largest operation, together with the 35-year extension in Armenia we obtained earlier this year, which is our second largest operation in terms of EBITDA, represents a significant increase in the average life of our portfolio and the major value creation for our shareholders.

Now please turn to Slide 5 for a review of passenger traffic trends. Total traffic increased 1.3% to 23.6 million passengers during the quarter. International travel continued to drive growth, increasing more than 6% with positive contributions from every country in our portfolio and particularly strong performance in Armenia. This more than offsets a decline of approximately 5.5% in domestic traffic, mainly reflecting lower volumes in Argentina and Brazil. Taking a closer look at the main markets. In Argentina, passenger traffic declined nearly 3% as international traffic growth of 3% was more than offset by a 6% decline in domestic travel, which remained affected by reduced airline capacity driven by the total suspension of Flybondi operations in early August.

Encouragingly, we saw a recovery in September when domestic traffic returned to growth after 7 consecutive months of declines, supported by additional capacity from Aerolíneas Argentinas and JetSmart. International traffic remained positive with long-haul travel growing nearly 10% in September. In October, domestic traffic was down 1.4%, still affected by reduced capacity, while international traffic grew modestly against October of last year. In Italy, passenger traffic increased just over 5%, supported by a strong summer season and growth in both international and domestic travel. International passengers, which accounted for more than 80% of total traffic, were up 6% with positive contributions from both Florence and Pisa Airports. Domestic traffic also improved, increasing 2% during the quarter.

This positive trend continued into October with total traffic growing 2.2% year-over-year. Brazil recorded passenger growth of just over 2%, driven by a nearly 15% increase in transit passengers, which more than offset softer domestic traffic, underscoring Brasília's position as a major domestic hub in the country. In October, overall traffic grew modestly 0.2% with transit passengers traffic more than offsetting a decline in domestic traffic. In Uruguay, traffic increased 4% with consistent growth throughout the quarter. We expect the launch of new international routes over the coming months to further enhance connectivity at Carrasco Airport. Traffic in October grew 1% year-over-year.

Armenia, our second largest market by EBITDA, delivered a very strong quarter with passenger traffic increasing nearly 17% and double-digit growth in each month of the quarter. This performance was supported by healthy demand from Europe, Asia and Russia despite the ongoing regional disruptions that affected traffic with the Middle East. The Wizz Air base continues to drive traffic growth with 3 additional routes announced recently, expanding the network to 22 destinations. This positive momentum continued into October with traffic growing 17% year-over-year. Lastly, in Ecuador, passenger traffic increased nearly 2% with international growth of more than 6%, offsetting softer domestic travel. International demand remained healthy, supported by additional routes and frequencies to the United States.

We also saw an encouraging improvement in September with total traffic increasing over 6% and domestic travel returning to a positive territory. In October, total traffic grew by 1.3% year-over-year with international traffic more than offsetting a decline in domestic traffic. In sum, across the portfolio, international travel was the key driver of passenger growth. We are also encouraged with the recent improvement in Argentina's domestic market and the continued expansion of airline connectivity across our operations. Turning to Slide 6. Cargo volume was broadly stable during the quarter, increasing slightly year-over-year, mainly driven by growth in Argentina and Brazil. However, an extraordinarily tough year-on-year comparison base in Argentina continued to weigh on total cargo revenues, which declined nearly 9%.

As discussed in previous quarters, temporary disruptions following a custom systems upgrade in September of last year resulted in longer cargo storage periods and exceptionally high storage revenues in the third quarter of 2025. We remain focused on improving profitability in our Argentine cargo operations through commercial and operational initiatives while continuing to capture opportunities across our broader cargo platform. Most notably, we have inaugurated 2 new courier terminals at Ezeiza and Carrasco airports in Argentina and Uruguay, respectively. I will now hand the call to Jorge, who will provide a more detailed review of our financial performance. Please go ahead.

Jorge Arruda: Thank you, Martin, and good day, everyone. Starting with the top line on Slide 7. Total revenues increased 14%, once again outpacing traffic figures, supported by strong commercial revenue growth of 22% with Armenia, Uruguay and Brazil delivering strong growth. Consolidated revenue per passenger rose 12% to $22.7 from $20.2 in the same quarter last year, reflecting stronger commercial performance across every country. Aeronautical revenues increased 6%, supported by broad-based growth across the portfolio, including Argentina. Brazil and Uruguay posted double-digit growth, while Italy also delivered a strong performance. Tariff increases in Brazil, Uruguay and Ecuador provided further support.

In Argentina, the increase in aeronautical revenues from higher international traffic more than offset lower domestic traffic and lower domestic passenger fees in U.S. dollar terms following the depreciation of the Argentine peso during the period. Commercial revenues were up 22%, well ahead of traffic performance, driven by growth across all countries of operation. Performance was led by fuel-related revenues in Armenia, together with broad-based growth in passenger-driven revenue streams, including VIP lounges, food and beverage, space rentals, duty-free and parking. This was partially offset by lower cargo revenues in Argentina. Importantly, excluding fuel and cargo, total commercial revenues were up 12% in the quarter. Turning to Slide 8.

Total cost and expenses increased 21%, primarily driven by higher fuel costs in Armenia, consistent with the continued expansion of that business, as well as higher salaries, mainly in Argentina. Excluding the fuel business, total cost and expenses increased 10%, mainly driven by the real appreciation of local currencies in Argentina and Uruguay against the U.S. dollar, which raised local currency operating costs in dollar terms. In Argentina, cost and expenses rose nearly 12%, reflecting the same effect as peso depreciation lagged inflation. Moving on to profitability on Slide 9. Adjusted EBITDA, excluding IFRIC 12, totaled $192 million, declining 1% as continued growth across all our other markets was more than offset by headwinds in Argentina.

Starting with Argentina, adjusted EBITDA declined 11% with margin contracting 5.5 percentage points, primarily reflected by lower domestic passenger traffic, lower domestic tariffs in U.S. dollar terms and an extraordinary strong quarter in 2025 for cargo revenues and the impact of the real appreciation of the Argentine peso on peso-denominated operating costs I mentioned earlier. In Italy, adjusted EBITDA increased 4%, YoY margin contracted 1.3 percentage points. Strong passenger growth and higher VIP lounge and food and beverage revenues were partially offset by a $1.3 million bad debt provision recorded in the quarter.

Brazil delivered adjusted EBITDA growth of 3%, driven by strong passenger growth, along with higher VIP lounge, space rental and parking revenues and further supported by the appreciation of the Brazilian real. Margin, however, contracted 5.5 percentage points, mainly due to a tough comparison base as the prior year quarter included positive one-off items totaling $2.1 million recorded in other operating income. Without such one-off items, EBITDA would have grown 19.3%. In Uruguay, adjusted EBITDA increased 12% and margin expanded nearly 1 percentage point, primarily reflecting solid passenger growth and higher cargo, duty-free and VIP lounge revenues. This was partially offset by the real appreciation of the Uruguayan peso, which increased our local currency costs and expenses.

Armenia delivered another strong quarter with adjusted EBITDA up 9% to $46 million, driven by passenger growth along with higher VIP and duty-free revenues. As in recent quarters, margin contraction reflected the continued expansion of the fuel business, which structurally carries lower margin than our core airport operations. In Ecuador, adjusted EBITDA was relatively flat with margin contracting 1.9 percentage points, supported by passenger growth and higher catering revenues offset by higher cost of services. Turning to Slide 10. Robust cash flow generation once again supported our liquidity position, which reached $840 million at the end of the quarter, 14% above the $715 million reported at the close of 2025.

Notably, all operating subsidiaries generated positive operating cash flow during the first 9 months of the year, except Italy due to Pisa's capital investment plan. Finally, cash used in financing activities primarily reflected the $148 million dividend paid to shareholders during the quarter as announced on our last call as well as debt repayments, mainly in Argentina. Moving on to debt and maturity profile on Slide 11. The total debt at quarter end stood at $1.1 billion, while net debt increased to $582 million from $502 million at year-end 2025. Our net leverage ratio remained at comfortable 0.7x, reflecting stable debt levels and continued cash generation.

Martin Francisco Eurnekian: Thank you, Jorge. Wrapping up, please turn to Slide 13. Our third quarter performance once again highlighted the benefits of our diversified portfolio with strong revenue growth and positive EBITDA contributions from all markets outside Argentina. We also maintained a solid financial position, providing the necessary firepower to continue focusing on new business activities. As discussed earlier, the extension of our Argentine concession agreement represents a major milestone for CAAP, strengthening the long-term economics and investment framework of our largest operation. We also made progress on several strategic initiatives. In Italy, we increased our indirect economic interest in Toscana Aeroporti from approximately 62% to 68% through the acquisition of an additional stake in the company in October.

This further strengthens our position in a market where we see attractive long-term growth potential. We also continue to work towards securing the remaining approvals for the Florence Airport Master Plan. At Pisa Airport, we recently inaugurated a new arrivals terminal, adding 7,100 square meters of space and creating additional opportunities to expand commercial activities. As Jorge mentioned, the successful $400 million debt issuance by Aeropuertos Argentina last month further strengthens our financial position and supports the execution of our investment program we mentioned earlier. On the expansion front, discussions in Angola continue to progress, and we are also participating in the tender process for Catania Airport in Sicily, Italy.

In Brazil, we recently inaugurated the Partage shopping mall at Brasília Airport, featuring approximately 40,000 square meters of leasable space and over 130 retail stores. This is another important step in developing additional revenue streams beyond traditional airport operations. Also at Brasília Airport, we have an important development ahead. Following an agreement with the Brazilian government to rebalance the concession, the existing fixed concession fee will be eliminated and replaced by a variable fee linked to revenues. Under this new framework, 100% of the concessionaire's shares will be auctioned on December 17. We will remain focused on this process, maintaining our disciplined approach to capital allocation, and we will communicate any developments as appropriate.

Looking ahead, we remain encouraged by international traffic trends across our portfolio. We are also seeing a recovery in domestic traffic in Argentina despite the disruption caused by Flybondi's suspension of operations, reflecting the resilience of underlying demand. However, scheduled runway maintenance at the Ezeiza Airport is expected to affect operations for 17 days during the fourth quarter, which temporarily impacted passenger traffic in Argentina. As discussed earlier, the increase in Argentina's domestic passenger use fee effective December 10 will also provide additional support to revenues going into 2027.

In Uruguay, we expect further revenue growth supported by the new instrument landing system, which began generating revenues in August, the recent opening of a significantly larger VIP lounge and additional cargo business initiatives, including the inauguration of a new courier terminal at Carrasco Airport. As we prepare for 2027, our focus remains on delivering operational improvements, strengthening commercial performance and pursuing growth opportunities. With that, we are ready to take your questions. Operator, please open the line for Q&A.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Your first question comes from the line of Alejandro Anibal Demichelis with Jefferies.

Alejandro Anibal Demichelis: Alejandro Anibal from Jefferies. A couple of questions, Martin, if I can, please. The first one is with the investments that you have agreed with the extension of the contract in Argentina, how much capacity do you think you can add to [ Aeroparque ] and Ezeiza with those $600 million of CapEx? That's the first one. And then on the second one, you mentioned some of those opportunities that you're seeing out there, Catania, Brazil, even Angola. So how are you thinking now on those opportunities in terms of size, in terms of returns that you can achieve in terms of balancing the portfolio?

Jorge Arruda: Alejandro, it's Jorge here. Thank you very much for your question. So starting with your first question on Ezeiza and Aeroparque . Following the investments, we -- the capacity of these terminals will go to, in the case of Aeroparque, 21 million and in the case of Ezeiza, 22 million passengers. That's considering the same shape of the peak hour that we currently have. So in practice, it could be a little bit more than that. So that's on the capacity side. On the opportunities, we are making very good progress on Angola in discussions with the government, in connection with the debt issuance associated with that particular concession.

And we will keep the market posted as we make concrete progress. Just today, we delivered a nonbinding offer to Catania in Sicily. And we hope in the next few weeks to have the first results and whether or not we pass to the second phase. Besides that, we've been very actively looking at a number of opportunities. They are, at the moment, not public. And again, we will keep the market posted as we make real progress on these opportunities.

Operator: Your next question comes from the line of Guilherme Mendes with JPMorgan.

Guilherme Mendes: I have 2. The first one is on a follow-up on the capital allocation and thinking about dividends as well. Given the contract rebalance in Argentina, free cash flow tends to increase even further starting in 2029. And even though you have this capital allocation opportunities, it seems the leverage might remain kind of relatively low. Just wondering if it changes anything on your dividend policy going forward or if the management would be willing or shareholders would be willing to distribute or increase the payout ratio? And the second one is a follow-up on the discussions in Florence.

If you can update us the next milestones and what to expect in terms of timing for the potential master plan in Italy?

Jorge Arruda: Guilherme, it's Jorge here. Thank you for your question. So in connection with dividends, in our March results, and prior thereto, in the Board meeting, we will be running the same analysis that we have done recently when we decided to pay the dividends of $150 million recently. And the analysis that we are going to run are composed of 4 main pillars: a balanced shareholders' return, keeping a financial strength of CAAP, maintaining an adequate cash balance at the subsidiaries for them to carry out their investment programs and their strategic objectives. as well as having the necessary liquidity or maintaining the necessary liquidity in CAAP to pursue its new business activity.

So based on those 4 main pillars, we will then decide whether or not to pay dividends and the amount. So in summary, that's our policy. Our policy is to -- is based on those 4 pillars. And at that point in time, with the then market environment and the situation at a point in time, we'll take a decision and obviously communicate to the market. In case of Florence, we continue to make progress. We continue to have a positive perspective. At this point in time, we have nothing more than that on a concrete basis to communicate, but we will keep the market posted as we make progress in this process.

Operator: Your next question comes from the line of Sofia Vatta with Latin Securities.

Sofia Vatta: Considering the fuel situation is affecting some airlines, do you see any impact on CAAP airports in the near term?

Jorge Arruda: So, thanks for the question. Look, it's obviously not great news that fuel costs remain under pressure. But so far, we have not seen any material impact in our operations in any part of the world because of the fuel costs. We will continue to monitor the situation. And if we see any material movement, we will keep the market posted. But so far, we have not seen.

Operator: Your next question comes from the line of Pablo Ricalde with Itaú.

Pablo Ricalde Martinez: Maybe thinking about traffic growth for 2027 in Armenia. You mentioned a bunch of new routes starting this December. What are you thinking in terms of traffic growth for Armenia in 2027?

Jorge Arruda: Thanks for the question. So I think we expect more of the same, positive trends across all markets, except Middle East, which will continue under pressure given the geopolitical situation in the region. So the feedback that we have, the number from the airlines, the numbers in the seat offer, et cetera, continue to point to a continued growth environment in Armenia. Obviously, we will continue to monitor the market, particularly the geopolitical situation in the Middle East and in the Russia-Ukraine situation. But aside from that, again, we continue to see a positive trend there.

Operator: Your next question comes from the line of Daniel Rojas with Bank of America.

Daniel Vielman: I was hoping to get some visibility into passenger traffic in Argentina [indiscernible] and I just wanted to get a sense of...

Patricio Esnaola: Sorry to interrupt. Can you speak slower and louder, please? It's very difficult to understand you.

Daniel Vielman: Can you hear me better now?

Daniel Vielman: My question was on Argentina and passenger traffic outlook for 2027 and how capacity is expected to come back into the market. I just wanted to get some color on what you're seeing for 2027.

Jorge Arruda: What we saw, Daniel, in the last 2 months, September and October is a more normalized volume in domestic passengers that as we reported and you may be very aware, was significantly impacted by Flybondi, which a year ago had approximately 15% market share. So what we saw on the domestic front is significant growth by Aerolíneas and JetSmart replacing the capacity of Flybondi. So that's one thing. And I think that's very important to bear in mind because Flybondi went down just a few months ago and a significant portion of their traffic has already been replaced by, again, Aerolíneas and JetSmart.

The other thing to bear in mind is that in October, we didn't see that in the numbers of the third quarter. But in October, there was days -- there was 7 days of a runway rehab that affected operations in Ezeiza. Nevertheless, international traffic in Ezeiza was basically flat in October. Going forward, we see modest growth in the low single digits.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] There are no further questions at this time. I will now turn the call back to Martin for closing remarks. Go ahead.

Martin Francisco Eurnekian: I want to thank everybody for your interest in our company. Remind you that our Investor Relations team remains available for you with any questions you may have, and please enjoy the rest of your day. Bye-bye.

Operator: This concludes today's call. Thank you for attending. You may now disconnect.

Ubisoft Montreal and Quebec are the latest to fall under the axe.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Ubisoft has laid off 166 people from its offices in Montreal and Quebec City, saying in a statement provided to PC Gamer that its "production needs are changing, requiring us to adjust the size of some teams to ensure our resources are aligned with current production priorities and business needs."

Founded in 1997, Ubisoft Montreal is one of the biggest game studios in the world, with roughly 4,000 employees as of 2022. Ubisoft Quebec, launched in 2005, is somewhat smaller but still substantial: Its website says the studio has "a team of over 600 creative, passionate, and innovative talents." The cuts reportedly impact employees who could not be assigned elsewhere after their existing projects were cancelled or rescoped.

Whatever the reason, it's bleak: This is the latest round in a series of layoffs that have battered the company since it announced a major restructuring in January, which followed years of struggles including workplace misconduct scandals, persistent difficulties getting games launched, and underperformance among big releases. Ubisoft said in today's half-year financial report report that it's reduced its headcount by roughly 1,100 people over the past six months.

Ubisoft did have a significant hit earlier this year with Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced, but that wasn't enough to prevent post-launch layoffs at developer Ubisoft Singapore. Ubisoft CEO Yves Guillemot said today that "Q2 exceeded our expectations, driven by the continued success of Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced and solid contributions from our broader portfolio."

Model ML raises $250m at a $1.5bn valuation for bankers’ AI agents

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Model ML’s annual recurring revenue grew 12 times in a year. Its CEO says the startup’s AI agents now help banks earn money, and are no longer just tools that save time.

Model ML co-founders Chaz and Arnie Englander in London.

Model ML, a London startup that builds AI agents for bankers, consultants and investors, has raised $250m at a $1.5bn valuation. General Atlantic and QED Investors co-led the Series B round, the company said on Tuesday.

Evolution Equity Partners, Airtree, Forestay Capital, Y Combinator, FT Partners, Latitude, Inception Fund and 13Books Capital also took part. The round takes Model ML’s total funding to $350m. FT Partners led its $75m Series A in November 2025, and HSBC Asset Management invested in August.

In the third quarter, Model ML’s annual recurring revenue grew 12 times year on year and its daily active users rose 26 times, the company said. It will use the money to grow its research and engineering teams in London and New York.

Brothers Chaz and Arnie Englander founded Model ML in 2023, Bloomberg reported. They previously co-founded Fat Llama, a rental marketplace that Sweden’s Hygglo bought for $41.5m in 2022, TechCrunch reported.

Each user gets an agent that runs on the firm’s own systems and data. When news breaks on a company a banker covers, the agent judges whether it matters and prepares the analysis in the firm’s formats. It works in tools such as Excel and PowerPoint to build pitch decks and due diligence reports.

“AI should do more than give you another answer or notification. It should understand your work and get things done,” said Chaz Englander, Model ML’s co-founder and CEO.

Model ML sends each task to the AI model that suits it best. A growing share runs on its own fine-tuned open-source models. On the work they handle, these cost 2% as much as frontier models and are in most cases five times faster, the company said. It added that customer data is never used to train AI models.

Banks have tied job cuts to AI. Barclays said in October it would cut 5,000 jobs, and Standard Chartered plans to cut 7,800 jobs in back-office roles by 2030. Englander played down the idea that Model ML is mainly a way to cut costs.

“The average age of a Model ML user is 33 years old, not 23 years old,” Englander told Bloomberg. “These tools are no longer productivity and efficiency tools. They’re revenue generative.”

Revenue is split fairly evenly across banks, consulting firms and asset managers, he said. Model ML’s rivals include New York’s Rogo, which raised $160m at a $2bn valuation in April. OpenAI and Anthropic also sell AI tools to financial professionals.

Apple’s head of camera and Photos software has retired, per report

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

According to a new report from Bloomberg, Apple’s head of camera and photo software retired last month. Jon McCormack spent eight years at Apple and “oversaw the Camera and Photos apps and the underlying technology that determines how users capture, view, and edit their pictures and video.”

Mark Gurman reports that Apple hasn’t named a new vice president to replace McCormack, who reported to software chief Craig Federighi. “Instead, his former deputies are assuming his responsibilities under other executives,” the report says.

In his role, McCormack oversaw “close coordination between software engineers and Apple’s camera hardware and chip teams.” He’s credited with last year’s redesigned selfie camera and other projects:

His work included the redesigned selfie camera introduced with the iPhone 17 lineup last year. This year, Apple added a variable-aperture camera to the iPhone 18 Pro lineup, giving users more control over lighting and depth of field. The company also introduced a customizable Camera app interface with professional controls.

Bloomberg describes McCormack as a “strong opponent of using generative AI to create or alter photographs,” a philosophy that created tension as Apple embraced AI tools in the Photos app. His retirement, however, “wasn’t related to a disagreement or clash with Apple over its AI strategy,” the report says.

McCormack spoke in depth about iOS 27 features like Clean Up, Extend, and Spatial Reframing in an interview with Tyler Stalman in June. He emphasized how the features “preserve the integrity of the moment.”

Xi-Trump-Putin summit: What did it achieve?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The presidents of China, the US and Russia have held an unprecedented summit. While Trump said the three leaders were "making history," Xi described the talks as "fruitful," and Putin called them "open and candid."

Chinese President Xi Jinping hosted US President Donald Trump and Russian President Vladimir Putin on Thursday for an unprecedented trilateral summit in Shenzhen, bringing together the leaders of the world's three most powerful military powers amid heightened geopolitical tensions.

Xi hailed the talks as "groundbreaking," saying discussions ranged from global governance and denuclearization to the war in Ukraine and energy security.

The "peace declaration" released after their meeting made no mention of Russia's war in Ukraine or the US-led war in Iran.

Instead, the three leaders agreed to pursue reforms of the United Nations, limit the role of the International Criminal Court and reaffirm their commitment to avoiding nuclear war. They also pledged to boost supplies of refined petroleum products to address global energy challenges.

The tightly choreographed meeting fueled concerns that Beijing, Washington and Moscow could seek to reshape the international order around competing spheres of influence.

While Xi said the talks with Trump and Putin were "positive, constructive and fruitful," Trump heralded the gathering as "really making history."

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

For Putin, the three-way talks, and his meetings with both leaders separately earlier, offered a chance to show the world that he was on par with the leaders of the US and China despite Western efforts to isolate him since he launched the full-scale invasion of Ukraine in February 2022.

Emma Chanlett-Avery, senior director for Political-Security Affairs at the Asia Society Policy Institute (ASPI), said Putin had the most to gain from the meeting as "he appeared on the world stage as one of the dominant players in the world."

Chim Lee, a senior analyst with The Economist Intelligence Unit (EIU), echoed this view.

The meeting itself is a "major outcome for Russia," he told DW, pointing to Russian media coverage that has cast it as "reinforcing Russia's standing in the world."

The joint statement also said the leaders are committed to work to reduce nuclear risks and prevent nuclear proliferation.

The last nuclear arms pact between Russia and the United States, known as New START, expired in February.

Trump has said he wants "a new, improved and modernized" deal that includes China.

But Beijing has so far been reluctant to accept limits as its estimated arsenal of around 500 warheads remains far smaller than those of Russia and the US.

Moscow and Washington are believed to possess more than 5,000 warheads each.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Lee said China is unlikely to participate in a trilateral institutionalized setting to control nuclear arms. "The gap between China's nuclear arsenal and those of Russia and US is still quite large. From China's perspective, it doesn't seem to make a lot of sense to put the three powers in the same bucket in this area."

The expert believes Trump attended the meeting as he is "actively thinking about his legacy in the final two years in office."

"So, in this context, the idea of setting up a trilateral nuclear arms control arrangement would have been a very big achievement," Lee said.

But, in the end, the declaration said the countries would merely engage in "dialogue" on the issue, avoiding any explicit commitments to downsize their nuclear stockpiles.

China initially hesitated to organize the trilateral summit as it did not want to be explicitly tied to Russia's war in Ukraine and harm Beijing's efforts to present itself as a long-term force for global stability.

The Chinese government, which is working to rebalance economic ties with the EU, was also wary of appearing to form a G3 club with Washington and Moscow, which could alienate other global partners.

Chanlett-Avery said that China had some degree of discomfort in convening this meeting, worried about getting involved in Ukraine issues.

But Beijing eventually "managed to get the outcome they wanted" and kept the European conflict out of the meeting's focus, she noted. "China was able to show off Shenzhen and all of its sparkling high-tech features. They convened a successful international meeting that grabbed lots of headlines," she added. "So, I think China probably came out feeling okay."

Prior to the summit, Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy had warned of a "second Yalta," referring to the Yalta Conference held in Crimea in 1945.

That meeting saw the Allied powers decide on the post-war European order following the end of World War II.

In Shenzhen, Putin stressed that Russia, the US and China, as victors in World War II and veto-wielding members of the UN Security Council, bore a "joint responsibility for maintaining international peace."

But Ying-Yu Lin, Associate Professor at Graduate Institute of International Affairs and Strategic Studies at Taiwan's Tamkang University, said the Yalta comparison "is a bit of a stretch," given the vast differences between 1945 and now.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Putin also said that all three leaders "agree that it's a useful format," and such meetings could take place again.

Lee, the EIU expert, said the trilateral setup "is unlikely to be just a one-off" and there will likely be more such meetings in the coming years. "Especially from the US side to have this kind of setup, there will be some interest in maintaining warm relations with both China and Russia," he underlined.

Buurman’s late goal earns Chelsea Champions League win over Paris Saint-Germain

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

To help deliver timely coverage of women's football, we may use AI tools to support content creation and editing. While we strive for accuracy and editorial quality, readers should independently verify important information.

Lucy Bronze supplied the assist on Wednesday 18 November as Chelsea broke a goalless deadlock with three minutes of normal time left in the fifth round of the league stage. Bompastor’s side had 71% of the ball in Staines but went in level at half-time.

The winner was the only goal of a one-sided evening. Paris Saint-Germain, coached by Paulo César, defended for long spells and were still level going into the closing stages.

The numbers show how much of the game Chelsea controlled. They completed 501 accurate passes from 578 attempted, while the visitors managed 174 from 238. Chelsea also won 10 corners to 3 and took 26 shots in total.

Fourteen of those attempts came from inside the box. The breakthrough still took until the dying minutes.

Bronze’s evening did not end with the assist. Two minutes after the goal she was booked for a foul, the only card shown by referee Maria Sole Ferrieri Caputi.

Chelsea put 14 shots on target, and Paris Saint-Germain made 7 saves to keep the score down. On expected goals, Chelsea’s 2.75 dwarfed the visitors’ 0.64, a gap that suggests the winning margin could easily have been wider.

The visitors were not without ambition. They had 17 attempts of their own, 4 of them from outside the area, and Chelsea needed 3 saves at the other end to protect their clean sheet.

Chelsea have now gone three games without conceding. Before this win they beat Manchester City 1-0 and drew 0-0 with Häcken, and they are unbeaten in their last six matches in all competitions.

The head-to-head record is just as one-sided. Chelsea have won all three meetings with Paris Saint-Germain, after a 1-0 win away and a 3-0 victory at home in 2022, scoring five goals and conceding none.

Paris Saint-Germain have won 2 of their last 6 in all competitions, against Le Havre and Marseille, and lost to Bayern Munich in that spell as well as here.

Both sides are back in league action quickly. Chelsea travel to Liverpool in the WSL on Saturday 21 November, while Paris Saint-Germain host Saint-Malo in Division 1 Féminine on the same day.

This Walter Salles-directed bio-drama made history last year, becoming the first Brazilian production to win Best International Feature Film at the Academy Awards. 'Dune: Part Three' Expands Its Universe, From the Liwa Desert to the Volcanic Shores of Iceland

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On Location peels back the curtain on some of your favorite films, television shows, and more. This time, we take a look at Dune: Part Three.

The celestial saga that is Frank Herbert’s Dune was practically made for cinema. And yet, for so long, adapting the breathtakingly expansive space opera seemed like something of a curse. David Lynch called his turn at the franchise “a huge, gigantic sadness” in his life, and for Alejandro Jodorowsky, his psychedelically expensive vision for Dune never escaped development hell.

On December 18, 2026, Denis Villeneuve’s Dune: Part Three arrives, overcoming the hurdles that had tripped up its predecessors and capping off the decade-in-the-making trilogy in an explosive finale. Key to the creation of the trilogy’s universe was keeping it as grounded as possible in ours, with otherworldly planets raised from the volcanic shores of Iceland’s beaches and the banded dunes of the United Arab Emirates’ Liwa Desert. By this point, fans of the first two installments are well-acquainted with settings like the desert planet of Arrakis, but Villeneuve's latest offering delves deeper than ever into their intricate textures. Condé Nast Traveler spoke with production designer Patrice Vermette, location manager Duncan Broadfoot, and director of photography Linus Sandgren to learn how this much-anticipated conclusion was crafted.

Dune: Part Three opens with Fremen forces landing on Enfeil, a wetland planet that stuns the soldiers—used to desert and drought—with its rain. After the battle concludes, the Fremen forces run to the black sand shores of the planet and wade into the sea. This sequence was filmed in Iceland, specifically at Ingólfshöfði Beach, a nearly 250-foot-tall cape and nature reserve. “Denis [Villeneuve] always envisioned a black surface going on for forever, a super flat surface with those low clouds and a band of light, just the sun piercing through the horizon," said Vermette, “Because of the black sand, we thought, obviously, Iceland. We also looked to see if it was possible to shoot in the Canary Islands.” At the end of the day, Iceland won the booking, leading the team to spearhead an unprecedented film shoot. “When we scouted Iceland, there were multiple options,” said Broadfoot, “but this particular beach that we ended up on is part of a nature reserve, and actually during the summer, you can book puffin tours, but it's never been shot on before, so that was also an appealing aspect.”

Twenty different locations across the UAE's Liwa Desert were stitched together to comprise Arrakis. Across all three films, the Liwa Desert has been used as a stand-in for the desert planet at the center of it all. “There's a reward to travel to the Liwa Desert, which is close to the border of Saudi Arabia, and the dunes there are like the kind you've seen in Lawrence of Arabia,” said Sandgren, “it's magical just to be there; they're beautiful and contain copper sand and beige sand, and the mix of the two creates these incredible patterns of ripples from the wind each night, and every morning, you see new patterns.” While Sandgren arrived at the desert for the first time to film for Part Three, for Broadfoot and Vermette, returning to the region was like a homecoming for the crew. “Denis [Villenueve] and Patrice [Vermette] are always very keen to find something new that we haven't filmed on before, so that was always the challenge," said Broadfoot, “specifically for Part Three, we had to go deeper and further into the desert than we had done in Part Two.” Filming in the ever-shifting landscape with temperatures peaking at a boiling 120 degrees Fahrenheit posed significant challenges to the shoot, especially as the crew based themselves even deeper into the desert, but getting comfortable was almost counter to the brief. “We love making movies because we thrive on challenges, you know?," said Vermette, "If it was easy, I don't think we'd want to make them.”

While nearly all of the Arrakis scenes were shot in the Liwa Desert, select sequences were set in Al Ain, the UAE's “Garden City.” Though scenes shot in Al Ain didn't take up as much screen time as those shot in the desert, they span a considerable range as the area served as the setting for Alia Atreides's personal Golgotha—the site where Paul's sadistic younger sister crucifies traitors to the regime—as well as where Chani plays with her young children. “It's an incredible area that is still very unknown,” said Vermette. “We shot on a mountain called Jebel Hafeet, the only real mountain in the area,” said Broadfoot, “we scouted the entire mountain and found a plateau up high, which gave you this sort of scale and rocky, craggy environment that's very different than the desert which we were filming in. It was a blank canvas for Patrice [Vermette] to take over and create his beautiful set.”

To enhance all the footage that was shot on location, several landscapes from Part Three were recreated using a combination of practical and digital effects across four facilities in Budapest: NFI Studios, HungExpo Budapest Congress and Exhibition Centre, Korda Studios, and Waberer. Much of the battle scene on Enfeil was shot in Budapest. “Most of it was in the back lot of Waberer,” said Vermette, “the amazingly technical thing achieved by Denis [Villeneuve] and Linus [Sandgren] was that they erected a 65-foot-tall by 470-something-foot-long LED screen wall, which was the biggest wall of that sort ever built." The sheer scale of the shoot even required the crew to break out cranes to navigate the airspace around production to move props and equipment around.

Atreides home planet Caladan has made an appearance in all three films of the trilogy. The visual opposite of Arrakis, Caladan was shot on the northwestern Kråkenes region of Norway. The agrestic shoreline is hugged by verdant cliffs and the frosty Norwegian Sea. Caladan's cameos in Part Three come as brief flashes, but Vermette also winks to the filming location in an Easter egg on Arrakis. “In the movie, in the interior courts of Alia's meditation room, there's a tree,” says Vermette, “I had the idea of putting a pine tree, a Norwegian pine tree there, because it's the roots of their family; it's something that anchors their family. The only pine tree in all of Arrakis is right there.”

As the most discerning, up-to-the-minute voice in all things travel, Condé Nast Traveler is the global citizen’s bible and muse, offering both inspiration and vital intel. We understand that time is the greatest luxury, which is why Condé Nast Traveler mines its network of experts and influencers so that you never waste a meal, a drink, or a hotel stay wherever you are in the world.

Fresha Becomes a Growth Engine for Beauty and Wellness Businesses, Connecting Partners With Customers Across the World's Leading Search and AI Platforms

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

LONDON, Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Fresha, the world's leading beauty and wellness booking platform, has announced the launch of the Fresha Partner Network, transforming its global Marketplace into a powerful growth engine that connects beauty and wellness businesses with customers across the world's leading search engines, AI assistants and digital platforms. Bringing together deeper and more advanced integrations across  Google Gemini and AI Mode, Google Search, Google Maps, ChatGPT, Claude, and other AI platforms and with Alexa joining in early 2027, the network enables Fresha partners to reach potential clients at the exact moment they're searching for services, turning online discovery into new customer relationships and confirmed appointments.

Building on the Fresha Marketplace, which facilitates more than 35 million appointments every month, the Partner Network extends Fresha's reach beyond its own app and website into the digital platforms where consumers increasingly begin their booking journeys. Whether someone is searching Google for a last-minute haircut, asking ChatGPT to recommend a nearby facial or exploring beauty services through an AI assistant, Fresha connects relevant, bookable businesses directly with that demand. Every Fresha partner is included automatically through their existing Fresha Marketplace profile, making their service menus, pricing, customer reviews, real-time appointment availability, professional profiles, service portfolios, images and other rich media accessible across supported platforms, without requiring additional setup or the management of separate integrations.

The commercial opportunity behind AI-powered discovery is already evident in Fresha's performance. Between January and October 2026, consumer traffic referred to Fresha through AI assistants increased by more than 640%, with ChatGPT accounting for the overwhelming majority of referrals. Fresha's daily impressions across Google's AI Overviews also more than doubled between early September and the end of October 2026. As AI-powered search becomes an increasingly important channel for customer discovery, the Fresha Partner Network positions beauty and wellness businesses to capitalize on this growing demand, connecting consumer searches with relevant services and real-time appointment availability.

A major component of the launch is Fresha's expanded integration with Google, bringing live appointment availability directly into Google Search, Google Maps and Google's AI-powered search experiences. Rather than simply directing consumers to an external booking page, Fresha provides real-time availability, allowing potential clients to explore available appointments and book directly from their search experience. By simplifying the journey from discovery to confirmation, Fresha helps businesses capture high-intent consumer demand, turning online visibility into confirmed appointments.

The network also builds on Fresha's pioneering integrations with ChatGPT and Claude, which have been live since August 2026. Between March and October 2026, ChatGPT-referred sessions increased more than sixfold to over 151,000 per month, while sessions containing a booking event grew 30-fold, highlighting the growing role of AI-powered discovery in driving booking activity. As the first beauty and wellness booking platform to introduce native integrations with both AI assistants, Fresha enables consumers to discover relevant businesses through natural-language conversations, with personalized recommendations informed by location, services, pricing, reviews and live availability. From finding a highly rated barbershop nearby to searching for a massage within a particular budget, these integrations connect consumer intent with real businesses and bookable appointments. Alexa's planned addition in early 2027 will further extend the network into voice-powered discovery.

For enterprise businesses, franchises and multi-location groups, the Fresha Partner Network provides a scalable customer acquisition channel across entire portfolios. As operators expand into new cities, enter international markets or open additional locations, each business's existing Fresha Marketplace profile automatically connects them to an expanding ecosystem of search and AI platforms. With services, pricing, reviews and availability presented consistently across supported channels, businesses can strengthen their digital presence, attract customers across multiple markets and manage appointments through Fresha's centralized platform, without additional operational complexity.

All Partner Network bookings are consolidated within Fresha Marketplace reporting, giving businesses a centralized view of appointments generated through the network. For enterprise groups, franchises and multi-location operators, this provides greater visibility into customer acquisition performance across locations and markets, helping teams monitor booking activity, identify emerging opportunities and make more informed decisions about business growth.

"The way consumers discover and book beauty and wellness services is fundamentally changing, and we want every Fresha partner to benefit from that shift," said Michaela Peicheva, Senior Product Manager at Fresha. "We're transforming Fresha into a global growth engine, connecting businesses with customers at the exact moment they're searching for a service. By extending our Marketplace across Google, ChatGPT, Claude and other leading platforms, we're making it easier for businesses of every size to attract new clients, fill their calendars and grow, without adding complexity to how they operate."

The launch of the Fresha Partner Network marks a significant expansion of Fresha's global Marketplace, establishing Fresha as the central hub connecting beauty and wellness businesses with the next generation of AI-powered discovery. As AI agents become increasingly embedded in how consumers search for and book services, Fresha's ambition is to become the go-to platform for AI assistants seeking trusted, real-time beauty and wellness information. By bringing together verified business profiles, services, pricing, reviews, rich media and live appointment availability within one connected ecosystem, Fresha is building a trusted, all-in-one infrastructure that powers intelligent discovery and booking across the world's leading digital platforms. With further integrations planned, the Partner Network positions Fresha at the center of an evolving AI ecosystem, connecting businesses with new audiences and turning consumer intent into confirmed appointments and long-term growth.

Fresha is the world's leading AI-powered booking platform for beauty and wellness, trusted by over 140,000 partner businesses across more than 120 countries. Its fully integrated ecosystem spans scheduling, payments, marketing, team management and a global consumer marketplace, giving beauty and wellness businesses everything they need to operate, grow and scale. The Fresha Marketplace connects millions of consumers with salons, spas, barbershops and wellness studios worldwide, with over one million monthly downloads and more than $1.4 billion in monthly transaction value. To learn more, visit fresha.com.

Anthropic shares jump 34% at open in record Nasdaq debut

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Claude maker opened at $111.50, a bigger first-day pop than SpaceX got in June.

Anthropic’s shares opened at $111.50 on Nasdaq on Thursday, 34% above the $83 IPO price, valuing it at more than $2T, per Business Insider. The company raised $107.3B with its IPO and a private placement, the largest listing on record.

Anthropic’s shares opened at $111.50 on Nasdaq on Thursday, 34% above their $83 IPO price, Business Insider reported. The jump, bigger than the 27% grey markets had pointed to, valued the Claude maker at more than $2T. The stock trades under the ticker ANT.

Dario and Daniela Amodei rang the opening bell with staff earlier in the day. By midday in New York, the shares had eased to about $107, still up nearly 29%, according to Bloomberg data.

The open beat SpaceX’s debut in June. SpaceX, which priced its IPO at $135 a share, opened 11% higher and closed its first day up 19%.

Anthropic raised about $81B by selling shares at $83 on Wednesday, above its $70 to $78 range. It is also raising $26B from a private placement at the same price, with Alphabet, Microsoft and Nvidia among the buyers. Together, that makes $107.3B, the biggest IPO on record.

The debut lifted the wider market, with the Nasdaq Composite up more than 1% after the open. As the first big pure-play AI stock, Anthropic is likely to be watched as a test of investors’ appetite for AI.

Not everyone is convinced. In October, research firm New Constructs said Anthropic might be worth as little as $144B. On Wednesday, a group of scientists signed a statement calling it “not a safe AI company”.

93% of Audited Merchant Accounts Missed Visa CEDP Discounts, Verisave and Canyon Payments Analysis Finds

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Report finds most audited accounts are still paying penalty rates and traces the losses to data-flow errors merchants can fix without switching processors

SALT LAKE CITY, Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- A year after Visa began enforcing its Commercial Enhanced Data Program (CEDP), many businesses that accept commercial cards are still paying higher interchange rates on transactions that could qualify for discounts. A new report from Verisave, a payments optimization and cost-reduction firm, produced with Canyon Payments, a payment services and integration partner, finds that 93% of 180 merchant accounts Verisave analyzed in a single month missed CEDP discounts. Together, they lost more than $800,000 in savings that month.

The report, "State of the Industry: Visa CEDP," finds that the payments industry moved quickly to adapt when Visa introduced CEDP in 2025, but the businesses paying the fees largely did not. Visa began charging a 5-basis-point participation fee on Level 2 and Level 3 volume in April 2025 and switched on automated data validation in October 2025. Level 2 rates for small business cards ended in January 2026, and Level 2 ended for corporate and purchasing cards in April 2026.

The penalty for falling short is steep. A $500 commercial or purchasing card transaction that qualifies for Visa's verified Product 3 rate costs about $8.85 in interchange (including the 5-basis-point participation fee). Downgraded to the non-compliant rate, the same transaction still incurs the participation fee and costs $13.60, an increase of 54%. Those downgrades land on top of the record $198.25 billion U.S. merchants paid in card processing fees in 2025, according to Nilson Report data cited by the Merchants Payments Coalition.

"A year in, the businesses most affected by CEDP are the ones that have done the least about it," said Jeremy Layton, Founder of Verisave. "Many finance teams saw their costs go up and assumed it was simply the new price of accepting commercial cards. Usually it isn't. The data is breaking somewhere between the merchant's systems and the processor, and that can be fixed without switching providers."

Some businesses have responded by passing the cost on to customers. One Verisave client that was losing nearly $500,000 a month to CEDP cited the program as the main reason it began surcharging card payments. The report notes a limit to that approach: card brand rules cap surcharges at 3%, and a business that misses CEDP rates is likely to end up with an effective rate above that cap. Surcharging alone can't recover the full cost.

The report finds that most failures are structural rather than the result of merchant error or fraud.

"In the accounts Verisave reviewed, 93% missed discounts they had already earned," said Canyon Payments CEO Alex Burgin. "We look at the payload, not the rate. When we open up the transactions, the failure is almost always structural: order totals that don't add up to the line items, commodity codes sent as plain text, Level 3 detail captured up front and dropped before settlement. That is not a pricing problem. It is a data problem, and once you can see exactly where it breaks, the fix is usually straightforward."

The full "State of the Industry: Visa CEDP" report is available at https://verisave.com/2026-visa-cedp-industry-report

About VerisaveVerisave is a payments optimization and cost-reduction firm that helps mid-market and enterprise businesses lower credit card processing fees without switching processors. Led by CEO Chris Lefebvre and founded by Jeremy Layton, Chief Commercial Officer, Verisave identifies setup errors, data-flow problems and misclassified transaction types that inflate processing costs, typically reducing fees by 10% to 30%. The firm has identified more than $3 billion in savings and works with clients including B&H Photo and GoDaddy. Learn more at verisave.com.

About Canyon PaymentsCanyon Payments is a payment services and integration partner for e-commerce and card-not-present businesses. Led by CEO Alex Burgin and CRO Jeff Hunsaker, who spent a combined 27 years at Visa, Canyon Payments helps merchants get more from the payment stack they already have, with zero-downtime switching and the integrations and custom development that fix the data problems behind downgrades. Canyon is a Certified Visa Acceptance Partner. Learn more at canyonpayments.com.

Trump’s accounts traded up to $97.5M in Nvidia stock, CNBC finds

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

One sale of Trump’s Nvidia stock came five days after Marco Rubio reportedly briefed him on Jensen Huang’s ties to a suspected Chinese spy. The White House says independent advisers run the portfolio.

Jensen Huang, then Nvidia’s CEO, speaks as President Donald Trump looks on at an ‘Investing in America’ event in the Cross Hall of the White House, 30 April 2025.

President Donald Trump’s accounts have bought or sold Nvidia shares at least 103 times since January 2025, a CNBC analysis found. Luke Fountain’s review of the financial disclosures, updated through August, values the trades at $27M to $97.5M in total.

The filings give dollar ranges rather than share counts. By the end of 2025, Nvidia was one of Trump’s three largest individual stock holdings, worth $12.5M to $58M. A year earlier, the stake was worth $615,000 to $1.3M.

One sale, of $500,001 to $1M, came on 18 May. That was the first Monday after Trump returned from his state visit to China, which Jensen Huang joined. Five days earlier, Secretary of State Marco Rubio had briefed Trump on intelligence about Huang’s relationship with a suspected Chinese spy, The New York Times reported.

The White House disputed the Times report without elaborating, the paper said. Trump denied being briefed when asked about it on 16 November.

“I haven’t heard that,” Trump said, according to CNBC.

He added that he did not know whether the woman, Alice Huang, was a Chinese spy. His accounts also bought smaller amounts of Nvidia stock in the days before and after the May sale. Trump disclosed more than 21,000 trades in 2025, CNBC found, against about 500 in his whole first term.

CNBC set Trump’s Nvidia trades against Huang’s growing access. The two have met or appeared together at least 15 times since Trump returned to office, by CNBC’s count. They first met at the White House on 31 January 2025. Two days earlier, Trump’s money managers bought $1M to $5M of Nvidia stock.

In August 2025, Trump confirmed a deal giving the US government 15% of Nvidia’s revenue from chip sales to China. A week later, his accounts bought $5M to $25M of Nvidia shares.

On 8 December, Trump said Nvidia could sell its more powerful H200 chips in China. His accounts had sold up to $1M of Nvidia stock four days earlier, and bought more the day after.

From January to August 2026, the accounts made 38 more Nvidia trades, worth $9.4M to $28.3M. On 8 October, Trump gave Huang the National Medal of Science. A disclosure released that day showed August purchases of up to $1.6M and sales of up to $1M.

A White House spokesperson told CNBC that independent advisers manage Trump’s portfolio without his input.

The arrangement aims to “avoid even the appearance of a conflict of interest,” Trump Organization spokesperson Kimberly Benza told CNBC.

CNBC noted that the disclosures offer no evidence that Trump directed the trades. Nor do they show that his advisers had access to classified intelligence. TNW has not independently verified the analysis.

Huang resigned as Nvidia’s CEO on 12 November. Trump has since backed his return, which Nvidia’s board is reportedly not considering.

Ubisoft cuts jobs at major Assassin's Creed and Far Cry studios as its year of layoffs continues

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ubisoft has confirmed more layoffs as part of its previously announced operational "reset", this time affecting 166 employees at its major Montréal and Québec City studios - the teams responsible for game series including Assassin's Creed and Far Cry. It says the cuts are necessary "to ensure our resources are aligned with current production priorities and business needs."

GamesIndustry.biz reports the cuts - believed to equate to around four percent of the studios' total headcount - will impact employees working on projects either recently cancelled or significantly changed.

The publication also understands the cuts, said to primarily impact production and support roles at Ubisoft Montréal and Québec City, include members of Vantage Studios - Ubisoft and Tencent's joint development venture announced last October, which focuses on the publisher's biggest franchises, including Far Cry, Rainbow Six Siege, and Assassin's Creed.

In a statement provided to Eurogamer, Ubisoft confirmed it is, "reducing its workforce by 166 positions across its Montréal and Québec City operations", adding: "As our portfolio evolves, our production needs are changing, requiring us to adjust the size of some teams to ensure our resources are aligned with current production priorities and business needs.

"We deeply value the contributions of these teams and recognise the impact this may have on our colleagues," the statement continued. "We are committed to supporting them through this process with care and respect. Those affected are being provided with severance, extended health care benefits, career transition services and other resources."

These latest job cuts are part of a "major organisational, operational and portfolio reset" announced by Ubisoft in January. That initial announcement was accompanied by the news of two studio closures - affecting its Halifax mobile and Stockholm teams - plus the cancellation of six projects, including the much-delayed Prince of Persia: The Sands of Time remake.

Since then, 105 jobs have been cut at Red Storm Entertainment, the Ubisoft studio behind the Ghost Recon series, and around 380 employees were reportedly laid off when the publisher's Winnipeg and Belgrade teams shut in June. In October, Ubisoft closed Trials developer RedLynx and downsized its Singapore Assassin's Creed: Black Flag Resynced team, impacting a total of approximately 120 employees. Amid all this, Ubisoft developers have been on strike in response to the publisher's sweeping layoffs.
