Walmart (WMT) Q3 2027 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Walmart's digital push and raised outlook highlight a shifting profit mix

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 19, 2026 at 12:43PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Walmart (WMT -0.68%) reported third-quarter results that reflected a continued shift toward digital commerce and services. Management stated that the company is leveraging its physical store network as a competitive advantage for fulfillment, enabling faster delivery speeds that drive customer conversion. The company introduced a new membership bundle in the U.S. designed to increase wallet share by combining supercenter, warehouse club, and streaming benefits. Management indicated that investments in supply chain automation and advertising are improving the profitability of e-commerce, which reached double-digit incremental margins during the quarter. The company raised its full-year guidance for sales and profit based on year-to-date performance and expectations for the holiday season.

Operator: Greetings. Welcome to Walmart's third quarter fiscal 2027 earnings call. At this time, all participants are in listen-only mode. If anyone should require operator assistance during the conference, please press star zero from your telephone keypad. I will now turn the conference over to Stephanie Wissink, Senior Vice President, Investor Relations. Thank you, Stephanie. You may begin.

Stephanie Wissink: Welcome, everyone. Joining me today from our home office in Bentonville are CEO John Furner and CFO John David Rainey. We will begin with highlights of the previous quarter and our outlook for the year. Then we will open the line for your questions. During the question-and-answer portion, we have invited Seth Dallaire, our Chief Growth Officer, as well as segment leadership to join: David Guggina from Walmart U.S., Chris Nicholas from Walmart International, and Latriece Watkins from Sam's Club U.S. So we can address as many of your questions as possible, please limit yourself to one question. For additional detail on our results, including highlights by segment, please see our earnings release and supplemental presentation on our website.

Today's call is being recorded, and management may make forward-looking statements. These statements are subject to risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially from these statements. These risks and uncertainties include, but are not limited to, the factors identified in our filings with the SEC. Please review our press release and slide presentation for a cautionary statement regarding forward-looking statements, as well as our entire safe harbor statement and non-GAAP reconciliations on our website at stock.walmart.com. That concludes my introduction. John, over to you.

John Furner: Good morning. Thank you for joining us, and thank you to our associates around the world for the strong results we delivered this quarter. We are proud of our performance, which reinforces that our strategy is working and driving growth. Globally, sales grew 4.4% and adjusted operating income grew 5.5%, both in constant currency. E-commerce was the primary driver again this quarter. We continue to gain market share, units and transactions were strong, and looking ahead, we expect a strong holiday season. As a result, we are raising our outlook for both sales and profit. We have spent years investing in our people-led, tech-powered omnichannel approach so we can better serve customers and members.

That starts with our everyday low price philosophy and our commitment to invest in price. This, combined with the strengths of our stores, clubs, e-commerce, and agentic experiences, shows our value proposition transcends technological change by giving customers more of what they want: great value, a broad assortment, and experiences that make their lives easier. We are seeing the benefits of this across the company. As shopping with us becomes more convenient, customers are turning to us more often. That grows our retail business and creates more opportunity for membership, marketplace, and advertising. As those businesses grow, they improve our economics and strengthen our ability to invest in the customer experience.

That is what we meant last quarter about the pieces powering each other, and that is why we are confident in the durability of our model. What encourages me is how our teams continue to build new capabilities, scale new ideas across markets and formats, and deliver consistent results. I see this when I spend time with our teams across our markets. Our associates are finding new ways to leverage our capabilities, connect more of our businesses, and bring successful ideas from one market to another. Now, let me talk about three strategic areas we advanced this quarter that show how we are getting more from our omnichannel capabilities. Those are membership, agentic commerce, and leveraging our assets. First, membership.

We introduced a new bundle that brings Walmart+ and Sam's Club Plus together at an incredible value, a lower price than purchasing the two memberships separately. We have spent years expanding our assortment and making shopping more convenient at Walmart, and Sam's Club has continued to refine its value proposition with great items, expanding the Member's Mark brand, and growing its fast delivery service. Each membership, Sam's Club Plus and Walmart+, is valuable on its own. Bundling them lets us do more for the same household while helping them save on the combined cost. We think about it this way: a delivery membership, a warehouse club, and a streaming service can add up to nearly $400 a year.

We are offering all that and much more for $120, the equivalent of $10 a month. Only Walmart can offer the broad assortment of a supercenter, a marketplace, a warehouse club, all at everyday low prices, and deliver it quickly. We are very encouraged by the early results, and we believe we are building the most essential membership in the United States. Our next steps in agentic commerce also build on our omnichannel foundation. We continue to see strong e-commerce growth, up 17% globally, even with the timing changes for Big Billion Days. In the U.S., Walmart was up 23% and Sam's Club was up 30%.

Our omnichannel model has enabled a high level of flexibility for our customers and our members, and we continue to find new ways to meet them where they are. Agents are beginning to play a bigger role in more shopping journeys, and we believe Walmart is well-positioned as these new ways of shopping develop. We are creating an environment where agents can help a customer discover new items and decide what to buy. Our omnichannel capabilities allow us to connect that digital experience to our locations, inventory, and the people who can fulfill the order, and at the prices people expect. We continue to add new capabilities to Sparky, our shopping agent.

An example is the ability to use voice and image as a shop. We added new languages like Spanish and French. In the U.S., usage grew 129% over last year, and we're seeing these baskets expand 40% as customers are able to find more of the items they want and need. We also recently launched Sparky in Mexico, Canada, and at Sam's Club in the U.S. We also announced new ways for customers to shop and stay in control when they choose to shop with their own agent. By assigning profiles to agents, customers will be able to set permissions and spending limits, see agent purchases separately, and turn access off at any time.

Our new Walmart Agent Gateway gives approved agents a better way to interact with us while helping protect customer information. Agentic terms set clear expectations for how agents must operate, including what agents can and cannot do. Our business, it's built on trust. Whether customers shop directly with us or through an agent, they should be able to count on accurate pricing, reliable availability, and clear delivery options. I've talked a lot about how we're bringing capabilities together to generate stronger growth and profitability, and we're also using our assets differently. Our stores, they're a great example. They are the key to proximity and the cost advantages we have.

When I was a store manager, we focused on driving sales inside the building, and today, our teams serve customers in the aisles while also fulfilling pickup and delivery orders, handling returns, and providing services. In many cases, the footprint is largely the same, but the volume moving through it is much greater, often double what it once was. We no longer see stores and e-commerce as separate, but connected parts of an integrated ecosystem. We can now reach 96% of U.S. households in less than three hours, and 60% in less than 30 minutes. We have a unique ability to get closer to customers, use inventory more effectively, and deliver with speed at a low relative cost.

Over 80% of e-commerce orders in the U.S. are fulfilled from stores, including our marketplace orders. By placing select marketplace inventory in stores, it brings more of our online assortment closer to customers and makes it available fast for local delivery. We can offer customers more choice while enabling our stores to support a larger marketplace business. We're also using our delivery capabilities to support fast and convenient delivery for quick-serve restaurants. We have a number of partners today and more to come. With a few clicks in the app, customers can get restaurant food delivery and a marketplace item along with groceries, pharmacy, and fashion.

Only Walmart can deliver on a promise like this, and we're moving fast to scale it. It doesn't stop there. We're looking across the company with that same omni-channel mindset, asking what else our assets can do for our customers, members, and shareholders. As I look ahead, I see a strong business with more opportunities to grow and a team that's excited to pursue that growth. We're earning more of our customers' business, expanding what our assets can do, and building capabilities we can take across markets. The opportunity is to keep bringing our advantages together in ways that improve both the customer experience and our economics.

We feel good about the results we're delivering today and our ability to create durable growth and long-term value for shareholders. I'll now turn it over to John David to discuss the quarter and our outlook in more detail.

John David Rainey: Thanks, John. I want to share my appreciation to all of our associates for helping us deliver a strong quarter with consistent sales growth and improved profitability. The durability of our earning streams is becoming more obvious. We're delivering on our annual financial framework of growing operating income faster than sales. We're also demonstrating greater flexibility within the model to manage through near-term factors that are outside of our control. We're pleased with how our teams are executing on behalf of our customers and members, and we're excited about where we're headed. I'll begin with highlights from our third quarter, and then I'll discuss guidance. There are three things I'd like you to take away from my remarks.

First, our business is strong. Our customer value proposition is resonating, and we're gaining share. Second, our strategic growth drivers are accelerating across the enterprise. E-commerce momentum is enabling strong growth in higher-margin commerce solution businesses while improving our business mix and profitability, and there's still a long runway of opportunity ahead. Lastly, we're delivering on our commitments to shareholders. Today, we're once again raising our sales and earnings guidance for the year. Now, turning to the financial and operational highlights for Q3. Enterprise net sales in constant currency grew 4.4% and exceeded the top end of our guidance of 3%-3.75%.

As we previewed on last quarter's call, we faced a 100 basis point headwind to top-line growth in Q3 due to the timing of Flipkart's Big Billion Days event, or BBD, in India, which will be reported in international's fourth quarter results this year. Walmart U.S. comp sales improved sequentially versus last quarter and increased 2.9%, including an approximately 70 basis point headwind from health and wellness, which I'll discuss in more detail shortly. Importantly, share gains in grocery categories were some of the highest in three years as we leaned into our customer value proposition with low prices and faster delivery speeds.

E-commerce momentum continues to be strong, up 23%, including nearly 50% growth in marketplace sales again this quarter. From a merchandise category perspective, grocery sales increased mid-single digits, while general merchandise sales increased low single digits. Our U.S. e-commerce marketplace is a key component of our long-term strategy. Momentum has been building in this business as more sellers join and we expand our product catalog with the brands customers want. It's important to note that within our comp sales disclosure, we include the commission and services fee portion of an e-commerce marketplace transaction, but not the overall value of that transaction, which is defined as gross merchandise value, or GMV.

Because our e-commerce marketplace is heavily indexed toward general merchandise items, this distinction can have a meaningful impact on reported comp sales. In Q3, Walmart U.S. general merchandise GMV grew mid-single digits, which was higher than the low single-digit reported comp sales for this category. This context reinforces the confidence we have in the health of the underlying business and the share gains we're seeing in general merchandise categories. We outlined last quarter that Walmart U.S. health and wellness sales would be pressured this year and next year due to ongoing impacts from maximum fair pricing, or MFP regulation.

Similar to Q2, MFP impacted the reported Q3 comp by approximately 125 basis points, which was partially offset by approximately 60 basis points of growth, primarily from higher GLP-1 sales. Importantly, this is specifically a sales headwind that does not impact profitability, as these branded pharmaceuticals have little margin benefit associated with them. Excluding health and wellness, the Walmart U.S. comp increased 3.6%, which is more representative of the strength of our business. As we did last quarter, we provided additional disclosure of underlying U.S. comp sales trends in our supplemental slide deck, which is on slide 14. Sam's Club U.S. sales increased 10%, with comps excluding fuel up nearly 5%.

International grew over 3% in constant currency, reflecting strong growth in China of 28%, partially offset by the impact from Flipkart's BBD timing shift. Enterprise adjusted operating income on a constant currency basis increased 5.5%, and adjusted EPS increased approximately 5%. This reflects improved incremental margins in our digital business, with strength in high-margin commerce solutions and the favorability and timing of Flipkart's BBD. As we outlined last quarter, our gross profit performance also reflects the investments we made in price and customer initiatives using the tariff refunds we received. We now have approximately 7,700 rollbacks across categories in the U.S. When looking at Q2 and Q3 together, underlying operating income growth increased double digits over that period.

From an operational perspective, we are making good progress optimizing our omni-channel fulfillment capabilities to provide greater speed. Digital is driving our sales growth, deepening our engagement with customers, and contributing to share gains across operating segments, merchandise categories, and customer cohorts. Something that people sometimes overlook is the role our stores play as fulfillment nodes in driving digital growth. Our stores are a key competitive advantage with their proximity to customers. They serve multiple purposes, including in-store shopping, as auto care service centers, pharmacy hubs, and digital fulfillment nodes. Between in-store shopping and digital fulfillment, we have more volume going through our U.S. stores than ever before.

In Q3, Walmart U.S. store-fulfilled deliveries increased over 40%, and expedited deliveries were up more than 50%, with stores being the last-mile fulfillment nodes for more than 80% of our e-commerce orders. Faster delivery speeds are leading to higher customer and member conversion. We continue to invest in our store network and explore additional ways to leverage this infrastructure to drive durable and profitable growth. Our last-mile network is another competitive advantage. As John mentioned, over the past several months, we have broadened our delivery marketplace to include over 2,400 quick-service restaurant locations, and we are off to a great start.

Our customers are loving the ease of ordering via the Walmart app, and these restaurant merchants are piggybacking on our existing delivery route densification at a lower cost. We also see these service partnerships broadening our total addressable market into new verticals. We firmly believe that our combined omni capabilities, stores with e-commerce at scale, alongside a large and engaged customer base, position us well to sustain our growth and share gains. In Q3, global e-commerce net sales grew 17%, led by the 11th consecutive quarter of 20%+ growth in the U.S. segment. Sam's Club e-commerce accelerated to 30% growth, with club-fulfilled deliveries up triple digits for the second consecutive quarter.

In our international segment, both Canada and China grew e-commerce more than 30%, which was partially offset by Flipkart. This momentum enables the growth of our commerce solutions businesses like marketplace, advertising, and membership. Walmart U.S. marketplace and fulfillment services had another strong quarter, with net sales up 49%, the third consecutive quarter with growth above 45%. Merchandise categories including furniture, hardlines, and toys all performed very well. Nearly 50% of marketplace GMV flows through Walmart Fulfillment Services, with units delivered same and next day increasing more than 60%. Faster delivery on a broader marketplace assortment continues to drive higher seller and customer conversion. Strength in marketplace has also been a driver of advertising growth.

As more sellers join the marketplace, they see sales increase by utilizing Walmart Connect advertising capabilities. U.S. advertising, including VIZIO and Vibe, grew 37%, led by strength in Walmart Connect, which was up 36%. Membership fee revenue grew 17% globally again this quarter, led by strong double-digit growth from Walmart+ in the U.S. and continued momentum in the Sam's Club businesses in China, Mexico, and the U.S. As John mentioned, we are encouraged by the response to our new U.S. bundled membership offering, with strong new member sign-ups and upgraded memberships running well ahead of our expectations. This was an important milestone in our journey to become the most essential membership program for consumers.

We know that mind share and wallet share grow when customers convert to members. Our members spend approximately four times more than non-members. This new membership bundle is expected to be accretive to the enterprise, but will impact the allocation of membership fee income and cost across the Walmart and Sam's Club U.S. segments. The brands will remain distinct, and as such, sales will continue to be reported in the segment where member spend occurs. Membership fee income and related benefit cost are split between the two segments based on the attributable value per member.

This has the effect of weighing negatively on the Sam's Club U.S. operating income growth while benefiting Walmart U.S. profitability until we anniversary the program launch in October of next year. Turning to incremental margins, the profitability of e-commerce continues to improve, even as we provide greater convenience and speed to customers. In Q3, Walmart U.S. e-commerce once again achieved double-digit incremental margins, aided by strong ad and membership revenue, further densification of our delivery network, and growth in fee-based expedited deliveries, and benefits in cost to serve from supply chain automation. We continue to prioritize growth investment, especially as we see attractive returns from projects across supply chain, stores, and technology infrastructure.

During the quarter, we issued approximately $6.5 billion in long-term debt at favorable rates. Our strong balance sheet and financial strength provide the flexibility to continue to invest in the business while providing strong returns to shareholders. Turning to guidance, we are raising our full-year enterprise sales growth outlook to 4.9%-5.2% from 4%-5% previously. For Q4, this implies enterprise sales growth in constant currency will be 4.5%-5.5%. There are several assumptions worth highlighting. First, the upward revision reflects the pass-through of year-to-date outperformance and assumes underlying sales trends will continue in Q4 with sustained share gains. Second, the timing shift of Flipkart's Big Billion Days event is expected to benefit Q4 sales growth by approximately 100 basis points.

Lastly, maximum fair pricing in the U.S. business is expected to be an approximately 125 basis point headwind to Walmart U.S. comp sales, partially offset by an approximately 50 basis point tailwind from GLP-1 sales, which is unchanged from our prior guide. For operating income, we are raising our full-year guidance on a constant currency basis to growth of 8%-8.6% versus 7%-8.5% previously, with implied Q4 growth of 4%-6%. If currency exchange rates stay where they are currently, we would expect a 70 basis point benefit to full-year sales growth and a 130 basis point benefit to operating income growth, with the implied Q4 impact from currency a net neutral.

As a reminder, we also expect cost related to the acquisition and integration of Vibe to be an approximate 20 basis point headwind to full-year operating income growth. We are raising our full-year EPS guidance to $2.84-$2.89 from $2.80-$2.87 previously. For Q4, that implies EPS of $0.73-$0.78. In closing, our teams continue to focus on what we do best, serving customers and members with everyday great value, exceptional convenience and speed, all while pushing our business model forward, diversifying our profit mix, and leading in agentic experiences. We are now happy to take your questions.

John David Rainey: Seth, it is a good balance between the three categories that you mentioned. I will start with the external pressures because it has already been talked about. Diesel prices have been, between diesel and transportation, it will be over $2 billion of incremental costs that we have had to navigate this year. So, we feel good about our ability to do that. We have also, as I noted in a prior answer, we continue to see this shift in the digital mix of our business.

Today, roughly 25% of our overall business is digital, is e-commerce. The way the expenses are allocated, that hits SG&A more than an in-store transaction. As you think about the next, call it five to seven years, our digital business is only going to continue to grow maybe another 10-15 percentage points over that period of time. So, you are going to continue to see that shift that is simply a reflection of the changing dynamic or changing mix of our business. You also hit on another really important point, and that is the intentional investments that we make. I would use Sam's as an example right now.

We are on a similar journey with Sam's in terms of our delivery network and what we are doing there around fulfillment as we were with Walmart U.S. a few years ago. So, I would characterize that as we are in more of an investment phase there, and we will continue to be. That is the luxury that we have as a business, to be able to be very intentional about these investments and still actually grow our profits faster than sales. We think, as I mentioned earlier, we have confidence in our ability to continue to do that. The importance of that, the takeaway from that, is that results in the durability of what you are seeing right now.

This is not something that is just happening in one quarter or one year, but we are making very intentional investments that allow us to continue this profit trajectory into the future.

John Furner: Seth, one of the other things to keep in mind is we operate in 19 different countries around the world, and throughout this year, as we have always, but I think we've really accelerated this year, is the idea that, first, innovation, great ideas come from our associates, and second, we can build things once and we can scale things globally. There's been a lot of progress in the year, and I see this accelerating over the next few quarters to leverage what we've done with platforms, everything from marketplace, fulfillment services, advertising, core operations, devices for associates, so they have the information they need in their hand across Canada, our market in Mexico.

So there are a lot of things that we have in place that we can scale and leverage and run on top of. The U.S. business, both Sam's and Walmart, have done a great job with efficiency and productivity, and more of that's to come to our markets around the world.

Operator: Our next question's from the line of Peter Keith with Piper Sandler. Please proceed with your question.

Peter Keith: Oh, thank you. Good morning, everyone. I wanted to look at the marketplace growth, which has been run-rating at roughly 50% growth the last three quarters, seeing meaningful acceleration in 2026. I was hoping you could unpack what you think are the key drivers to this recent growth so we can understand, I guess, the sustainability as we look forward for a couple of years. Things like the Who Knew? ad campaign come to mind to me, as well as adding new suppliers. But I'm hoping you can provide some more detail.

Seth Dallaire: Yeah, thanks for the question. In terms of marketplace growth, I think that a lot of the growth comes from just the increase in assortment and our efforts to bring in more sellers into the platform, drive more assortment, make it more available to our customers and members through our e-comm offering. That's been really helpful in terms of when that assortment is exposed to customers and members. It's oftentimes new, and it's novel to them. They buy it. That encourages more sellers to come in and give us more assortment.

I'd also say that some of the innovation that John referred to related to forward deploying marketplace items into our supercenters and clubs is a new approach that we're taking where we think that as we get that seller assortment closer to customer homes and can ride on the rails of all the investments we've made in Express Delivery, which we know our customers and members love, that we should see continued acceleration in that business and that we're able to serve sellers in ways that maybe we haven't in the past.

Operator: The next question's from the line of Scot Ciccarelli with Truist. Please proceed with your question.

Scot Ciccarelli: Good morning, guys. Thanks for the time. I know it's still early, but there are some concerns in the investment community around the value of advertising in an agentic-dominated world. Can you help us understand how you plan to protect your advertising earnings in an agentic world, especially since one of your largest competitors out there is basically barring third-party agents from its site? Thanks.

Seth Dallaire: Sure. Thanks for the question. We see our strategy with engaging with agentic commerce and leaning into the opportunity as one where we can learn a ton. Firstly, we want to be where our customers and members are. If they are engaging with these agents and shopping via those agents, then we think we continue to serve them and do so in ways that are not only great for our business, but maintain the values that we have had in serving customers through our physical supercenters. But in terms of the advertising components and the way that customers and members are shopping in these agents, we are still learning a ton.

Conversational commerce and the way that people are creating queries and prompts in these agents is quite different than what we have seen historically. But we are learning about those prompts and how to serve customers and allow marketers and brands to get their products in front of customers. What has not changed in customer behavior, even through agentic shopping, is the desire to learn about new products and discover new products and brands. That has been consistent throughout shopping, whether it is digital, physical, or agentic. We believe that our strategy right now is allowing us to learn about that and serve members, and we will continue to do so. An important part of that is brand advertising.

We are looking at that and being aware of how we are enabling that in these new environments.

Operator: The next question is from the line of Krisztina Katai with Deutsche Bank. Please proceed with your question.

Krisztina Katai: Hi. Hi, team. Good morning, and thanks for taking the question. I wanted to ask on capacity. As e-commerce continues to grow 20%+ and 30-minute delivery scales, is there a point where the supermarket just becomes a constraint versus an advantage? How are you evaluating dark stores or dedicated fulfillment nodes in higher density markets? What would need to be true economically for that model to make sense? Thank you.

John Furner: Hey, Krisztina, thanks for the question. Let me start what I spoke about a couple minutes ago. Being in 19 markets around the world, we have a lot of opportunity inside the company and looking outside the company to learn about the most effective ways we can serve customers. A few examples, in India, we have Flipkart Minutes where the average transaction time, Chris, is 13 to 14 minutes. It is small dark stores, easy to scale quickly. They have scaled really quickly, and it has been impressive what they have done in our business.

In China, in particular, Sam's Club led with cloud warehouses a few years ago, which is a great model because it takes the head of the assortment, the top of the assortment, it forward deploys it near the clubs, but it enables customers, members in that case, to offer the best part of the top of the assortment, meals, think center plate, and have it delivered in a short time. Last year in the U.S., we opened what we would call Walmart Depots, which are also expanding. We have some examples of where we put those markets. They do more than just the top of the assortment here in the U.S.

They are also delivery stations for e-commerce orders, so it is forward deployed inventory, but it is also a way that we can get, as David said earlier, a more dense network with our drivers. We will continue to learn around the world, and we are going to expand the way we deliver from stores, from fulfillment centers, the way that stores and depots are flow-throughs. One thing I can say about this team for sure is, they are innovative, and they will find ways to expand our reach. David mentioned a few minutes ago that 96% of U.S. households, 130 million roughly households, are now reachable within three hours and 60% under 30 minutes.

We will continue to push, and if we need to invest in more capacity, we will, we are, and we will continue to find ways to grow for our customers.

Operator: Thank you. Our final question is from the line of Peter Benedict with Baird. Please proceed with your question.

Peter Benedict: All right. Good morning, guys. Thanks for sneaking me in here. First, a follow-up and then a question. A follow-up kind of on the agentic commerce impact on advertising question that was asked earlier. You did a nice job addressing kind of the retail media side of that. I am just curious if there is anything to add there in terms of what agentic might mean for the VIZIO business or the Vibe.co business. Are those different, and what are the opportunities there in an agentic world, if they are impacted at all? My main question is really around expanding these commerce solutions beyond the core U.S. business. John, you talked about taking Sparky into Mexico and Canada.

I am just curious kind of your view, bigger picture, and opportunities to do things like that within the franchise over time. Thank you.

John Furner: Yeah, Peter, great question. We are excited about expansion of these platforms across international markets. I mentioned a few things earlier, but we are definitely thinking about advertising data services as one team across our markets, in particular North America. We have had exciting launches with Sparky in Canada and Mexico. Walmart+ was also a fast follow for those markets. The third is the talent we have in the company. We have a lot of associates who have worked in Mexico or the U.S. or in Mexico or expats in Canada, vice versa.

We are trying to think about this as a business that does not necessarily always see the geographic borders, but rather sees a way that capabilities and our capacity can expand to other markets so we can accelerate. Let me turn it over to Seth to pick up on VIZIO and Vibe and the opportunities.

Seth Dallaire: Yeah. In terms of the agentic capabilities there, we are looking at it in two dimensions. One would be a consumer-facing or TV owner dimension. There is a lot of really interesting things that we are experimenting with as it relates to that user experience on the TV that may relate to search and discovery of content programming, and other ways that we can serve content through the VIZIO operating system. Secondly, as it relates to Vibe, we use a lot of agentic capability within the technology in Vibe to power all of the offerings that are available for that small, medium-sized advertiser that we are chasing through the connected TV space.

It is not only agentic as it relates to the consumer experience, but it is also agentic capability that we are using on the actual machinations of how the platform works for the advertisers that are buying advertising with us.

Operator: Thank you. This now concludes our question and answer session. I would like to turn the floor back over to John Furner for closing comments.

John Furner: But first, just like to thank everyone for the time and the interest, the questions this morning. I have three points. The team at Walmart and around the world, I am really proud of their performance. Our associates in stores, clubs, distribution centers, our offices have momentum, and I am really, really grateful for the way that they are focusing on growth, value, and they are executing in a disciplined way. The second is, it is also exciting to see some of these capabilities coming to life across markets. I love the innovation, whether it is agentic commerce, the announcement on membership. We are really bullish on the possibilities this can create for members over time, things like food delivery.

We are able to do those things in a creative, innovative way without in any way distracting our focus on the customer and what we do in our retail business. And then number three, it is the holiday season. It is game time. So we are focused on delivering for our customers for the holiday meal, for gift-giving. It is a busy time for a lot of people all around the world, and we will be ready to take care of them as we get into the busiest part of the year. So thanks again for your interest, and we will talk to you soon.

Operator: Ladies and gentlemen, thank you for your participation. This does conclude today's teleconference.

Bath Body Works (BBWI) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Digital momentum returned while store traffic softened.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 19, 2026 at 12:43PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Bath & Body Works (BBWI +3.08%) reported third quarter results that exceeded its internal guidance ranges, supported by two consecutive quarters of digital growth and the scaling of new product franchises. Management reported that the company is one year into its multiyear Consumer First Formula strategy, which focuses on product innovation, brand reignition, and marketplace expansion to return the business to sustainable growth in 2027. While store traffic and seasonal collections like Halloween faced pressure, the company raised its full-year sales outlook and announced a new $500 million share repurchase program. The company is currently focused on navigating a competitive holiday environment while offsetting inflationary and tariff-related margin pressures through its Fuel for Growth efficiency program.

Operator: Good morning. My name is Melissa, and I will be your conference operator today. At this time, I would like to welcome everyone to the Bath & Body Works Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. Please be advised that today's conference is being recorded. During the question-and-answer portion, you may ask a question from the phone by pressing star one. I will now turn the call over to Luke Long, Vice President of Investor Relations. Luke, you may begin.

Luke Long: Good morning, and welcome to Bath & Body Works Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. Joining me on the call today are Daniel Heaf, Chief Executive Officer, and Tom Javitch, Interim Chief Financial Officer. In addition to this call and this morning's press release, we have posted a slide presentation on our website that summarizes the information in these prepared remarks and provides related facts and figures regarding our operating performance and guidance. As a reminder, some comments today may include forward-looking statements related to future events and expectations. For factors that could cause the actual results to differ materially from these forward-looking statements, please refer to the risk factors in Bath & Body Works 2025 Form 10-K.

Today's call also contains certain non-GAAP financial measures. Please refer to this morning's press release and supplemental materials for important disclosures, including reconciliations to the most comparable GAAP financial measure. With that, I will turn the call over to Daniel.

Daniel Heaf: Thank you, Luke, and good morning, everyone. Today, I will review our third quarter results, our progress with the Consumer First Formula, and how we are preparing for holiday. Our third quarter results exceeded our expectations. Net sales declined 1%, ahead of our guidance range of down 5% to down 2.5%. Adjusted earnings per diluted share were $0.26, above our guidance range of $0.07-$0.12. One year ago on this call, we introduced the Consumer First Formula, our plan to return Bath & Body Works to sustainable growth. Over the last 12 months, our passionate team has executed with speed across all four pillars: product, brand, marketplace, and efficiency. We are beginning to see that work show up in company performance.

Let me give you a few examples. Digital sales trends have improved quarter-by-quarter, moving from decline to growth, supported by stronger conversion and accelerating new customer recruitment. Customers are responding to our innovation: Reserve, Fruit Fusion, Essentials, and the reimagined Everyday Luxuries collection. Our focus now is to grow these franchises into a larger part of the business. Strong execution by our international team and partners has driven consistent high single-digit or better retail sales growth throughout the year. Early results from our expanded distribution channels suggest we are attracting new customers and winning a greater share of existing customers' spending. We are one year into a multi-year transformation, and progress will not always be linear.

We are on track, but far from satisfied. This year, we have put many of the building blocks in place. Our next task is to scale what is working and turn this progress into durable growth. Let me turn to what we learned in the third quarter and how we are approaching the balance of the year. Our strongest moments in the quarter came when great fragrance, great quality, and great value came together. Based on our third quarter performance and outlook for the balance of the year, we are increasing our full-year net sales guidance to down 3.4% to down 2.1%, and we are holding our adjusted earnings per diluted share guidance to $2.60-$2.80.

Holiday is our most important selling period, and retail traffic remains under pressure. Inflation continues to weigh on consumers, and competition in our core categories continues to intensify. Against that backdrop, we are taking a prudent approach to the outlook. Looking further ahead, our goal remains clear: return the business to sustainable growth in 2027. Let me now turn to the work behind that goal. First, creating disruptive and innovative products. We listen closely to consumers and move quickly to turn what they want into quality products and franchises that deliver extraordinary value. A franchise is more than a single launch. It is a platform we can grow across fragrances, formats, categories, channels, and markets.

The third quarter brought our first wave of this innovation. Essentials addresses a clear consumer need: body care for sensitive skin with soft, close-to-the-skin fragrance. The range combines hypoallergenic, dermatologist-approved formulas across a replenishing body wash, ultra-hydrating body lotion, and eau de parfum. This is the Consumer First Formula in action, combining the emotional power of fragrance, formulas that perform, modern design, and exceptional value. To support the Essentials launch, we partnered with board-certified dermatologist, Dr. Michelle Henry. She reinforced the benefits and the ingredients and helped consumers build a routine for sensitive skin. Sales have exceeded our expectations, and customers are coming back.

More than half of Essentials buyers return to shop within 30 days, significantly above our brand average. Everyday Luxuries meets consumers' desire for exceptional fragrances at accessible price. We reimagined the collection with higher concentration formulas and elevated packaging. New forms, including rollerballs and eau de parfum, give consumers more ways to make these fragrances part of their daily routines. These new formats are adding sales to the collection without reducing sales of our core fine fragrance mists, which continue to perform well. The response has exceeded our expectations, with retail sales of the reimagined Everyday Luxuries collection approximately doubling versus the comparable period last year. The collection is also attracting younger and more diverse consumers.

Our Reserve collection was created for consumers who want fragrance to be part of their home décor. The idea is scent as design, sophisticated fragrances paired with ceramic and glass pieces designed to be displayed. Since launching in August, the four-wick candle has been the collection's leading format, and early repeat purchase is running above our brand benchmark. Together, the new franchises are gaining traction and contributing mid-single-digit sales while also achieving the premium average selling prices we planned. Customers shopping the franchises are returning to shop with us at rates above our company average. Some of the new products have sold faster than planned, and limited availability has prevented us from fully meeting demand.

Our priority is to improve availability so we can turn more of that demand into sales. Our ambition is for this portfolio, including planned new launches, to reach a high single-digit share of global retail sales in 2027. This is our first wave of innovation. There is much more to come across our core categories in 2027. Alongside building our new franchises, we are strengthening our seasonal collections. Preserve brought fall to life across body care, home fragrance, and accessories, pairing new fragrances, including Wild Blackberry Jam, with returning favorites. Elevated products, thoughtful details, and rich storytelling captured the mood of the season, and the collection performed well. Our seasonal performance, however, was uneven.

Halloween fell short of our expectations, and we are now applying the lessons from Preserve to our seasonal planning, bringing the assortment, presentation, and storytelling together around what consumers want from each season. Growing our franchises and strengthening our seasonal execution are both essential to returning the business to sustainable growth. Next, reigniting the brand. Our brand belief is simple: everybody deserves to feel good. We are building a modern demand engine to reach more consumers and give them more reasons to shop. In the third quarter, we increased marketing investment and brought our product launches and storytelling together more consistently.

Celebrity partnerships, creators, targeted advertising, and our own channels are helping more consumers discover and engage with Bath & Body Works. In Q3, customer trends continued to improve. Existing customers had their strongest quarter of the year, and we saw better retention and reactivation trends. New consumer recruitment is also improving, with digital showing progress first, consistent with where we expected to see the benefits of the Consumer First Formula emerge. In stores, we know we have more work to do to give both new and existing customers more reasons to visit. This holiday, Zooey Deschanel brings joy and nostalgia to our Season of Wonder campaign.

Model and actress Valentina Ferrer brings new energy to A Thousand Wishes, one of our most iconic fragrances. You will see the campaigns across TV, TikTok, YouTube, Instagram, and Facebook. Already, the campaign for A Thousand Wishes is one of our top-performing social posts of the year. Early brand lift research is encouraging. We are seeing these campaigns drive improvements in key brand metrics, including brand love and perceptions of high-quality ingredients. On TikTok, where we reach our highest concentration of new, younger customers, we are seeing encouraging gains in brand awareness. Alongside these campaigns, we are continuing to expand our relationship with creators. We are building relationships with thousands of creators, from emerging voices to highly influential partners.

They help consumers discover products where they spend their time and keep Bath & Body Works part of the shopping conversation. Early results show creator content generating click-through rates far higher than branded content. This demonstrates the ability of creators to help more consumers and engage with Bath & Body Works. Third, winning in the marketplace. Our strategy remains consistent, to make Bath & Body Works easier to discover and easier to shop wherever consumers choose to buy. In the third quarter, digital delivered its second consecutive quarter of growth. Conversion improved, and we again saw growth across new, existing, and reactivated customers in our digital channel.

During the quarter, we fully relaunched our website with richer brand and product storytelling, AI-powered browsing, and personalized product recommendations. As we said last quarter, we expect digital to be one of the first places where the Consumer First Formula would show results. We are pleased to see this continued improvement and early proof point in our strategy. At the same time, we know there is so much more we can do to make our digital experiences more competitive and more enjoyable for consumers. Expanded distribution is about putting our product in the path of consumers wherever they choose to shop. Our goal is to bring new consumers to the brand and re-engage those who have lapsed.

Amazon and Ulta extend our reach, and both continue to build momentum in the third quarter, with improving sales trends and encouraging new customer recruitment. At Ulta, we are further expanding our reach for holiday, bringing a broader assortment to approximately 1,500 stores, up from approximately 600, and launching Pink Suede exclusively in the channel. We are learning quickly and see room to refine the assortment, improve availability, and drive higher sell-through. Ulta is an outstanding partner, and their spring bookings are encouraging. Together, we see a significant opportunity to build a large, profitable, and enduring business. We are expanding with discipline.

Our goal is to reach consumers we would not otherwise reach while protecting the strength and economics of our own stores and websites. The early evidence reinforces our confidence in this approach. Our own stores remain at the heart of our brand, and their performance is not yet where we want it to be. The changes we made in the quarter are improving conversion, but we have more work to do to give consumers more reasons to visit. Our focus is on helping consumers discover more of our products and bringing the brand to life through stronger storytelling and more engaging experiences.

International continues to deliver consistent growth and represents an attractive asset-light opportunity to extend the reach of Bath & Body Works globally. In the third quarter, international retail sales grew low double digits year-over-year. Our franchise partners continue to expand their store footprints in existing markets, supported by attractive unit economics and strong consumer engagement. At the same time, we see meaningful opportunities to enter new markets. Brazil is a great example. We opened our first stores there this year, and initial consumer response has been outstanding. International has a long runway for growth, with significant white space in both new and existing markets.

Through the Consumer First Formula, we have put important pieces in place this year: new product franchises, a relaunched website, and new ways for the consumer to shop with us. Our focus in 2027 is to build their contribution while improving performance in our stores. That is how we intend to turn these early wins into sustainable growth. Fourth, operating with speed and efficiency. Through Fuel for Growth, we are ahead of our plan and expect to deliver approximately $200 million in savings this year. Those savings, together with broader operational improvements, help fund product innovation, marketing, digital capabilities, and expansion into new channels. We are simplifying how we work so we can move faster and invest in growth.

Stepping back, the third quarter provided tangible evidence that the Consumer First Formula is working, but we will not be satisfied until we return the business to consistent growth. I want to thank all of our associates for the care they show our customers and the hard work behind our progress. I am especially grateful for the energy and commitment they are bringing to holiday. All of us at Bath & Body Works are focused on returning the business to sustainable growth in 2027. With that, I will turn the call over to Tom.

Tom Javitch: Thank you, Daniel, and good morning, everyone. Today, I will review our third quarter results and provide our fourth quarter and updated fiscal 2026 guidance. Beginning with the third quarter, net sales were approximately $1.6 billion, a decline of 1% versus last year, and above our guidance range of down 5% to down 2.5%. Underlying business performance in the quarter remained pressured, but results exceeded our guidance and improved sequentially compared to Q2. Looking at our performance by category. First, across all categories, we experienced growth in new and existing franchises, which were about equally offset by a decline in Halloween seasonal products, inclusive of collaborations. Body care performed well below our expectations, but consistent with second quarter performance.

There were encouraging results in our relaunch of Everyday Luxuries late in the quarter, and a continued positive response to our innovation, including Fruit Fusion and Essentials. This was offset by weaker overall performance in our Halloween and seasonal stories. Home fragrance was about flat in the quarter. Candles saw continued growth in single wicks and the introduction of our Reserve four-wick candles. Other highlights include growth in our Wallflowers heaters and our concentrated room sprays. Soaps and sanitizers increased low single digits, with continued strength in sanitizers, supported by our newest form, the larger flat-back pocket spray.

In U.S. and Canadian stores, net sales were approximately $1.2 billion, a decrease of 4% versus last year, and slightly better than first-half performance. Store sales were pressured by a few factors. As discussed on previous calls, the introduction of Free Ship 50 in the fourth quarter of 2025 drove some expected channel shift to direct. We will fully lap this by the end of the fiscal year. Traffic remains a headwind, particularly late in the quarter when we saw external traffic benchmarks weaken due to a tougher overall consumer environment. We began piloting a series of store activations and events in Q3, focused on driving customer engagement around newness, seasonal moments, and compelling value.

We are learning from these tests and are applying these learnings to our go-to-market plans for Q4 and fiscal 2027. Direct channel net sales were $307 million, an increase of approximately 3% versus last year. As Daniel highlighted in his remarks, we are investing in digital capabilities and seeing promising early results. International and other, inclusive of our expanded distribution wholesale revenues, net sales were $101 million, an increase of about 39% versus last year. These businesses contributed approximately 190 basis points of overall growth in the quarter. This third quarter contribution is approximately 150 basis points higher than the expected fourth quarter contribution, reflecting wholesale shipments ahead of the holiday peak season.

International retail sales were up low double digits, and international net sales were up even higher, both above expectations. The higher net sales were driven by new store and new market openings and an improved ability to ship into the Middle East. Our third quarter adjusted gross profit rate was 40.8%, a decline of approximately 50 basis points compared with the prior year, and above guidance. Adjusted merchandise margin rate improved by approximately 30 basis points, while adjusted buying and occupancy deleveraged on lower sales and investments into real estate. The merchandise margin improvement reflected a combination of favorable physical inventory results and timing of Canadian tariff expense shifting out to Q4. Mix-adjusted AUR was roughly flat in the quarter.

Adjusted SG&A was $522 million, and adjusted SG&A rate was 33.1%, reflecting increased marketing investments and deleverage on lower sales. Bringing it all together, adjusted operating income was $121 million, or 7.7% of net sales, and adjusted earnings per diluted share were $0.26. Inventory ended the quarter at approximately $1.3 billion, up approximately 2% versus last year. We are investing behind key franchises and forms in Q4 and projecting inventory to be up meaningfully at the end of the year to support our assortment initiatives for 2027. Moving to real estate. During the quarter, we opened 24 stores, primarily off-mall, and closed two stores. International partners opened 29 new stores and closed two stores.

We ended the quarter with 1,959 North American company-owned stores, with approximately 60% of our fleet in off-mall locations, and 623 international locations. Importantly, we opened our first three stores in our newest design concept and will continue to test and learn into next year. This concept presents an elevated and immersive experience, the goal of which is to give both existing and new consumers more reasons to visit and explore and engage with the brand. Turning now to the fourth quarter. We expect fourth quarter net sales to be down 5.5% to down 2%.

Gross profit rate is expected to be approximately 42.4%, reflecting merchandise margin pressure driven by incremental Canadian tariffs, the impact of higher crude oil rates, and buying occupancy deleverage on lower sales and investments in real estate. SG&A rate is expected to be approximately 25%, reflecting net sales deleverage and continued investments in the Consumer First Formula, primarily marketing. Net non-operating expense is expected to be approximately $51 million. The tax rate is expected to be approximately 25.7%, and weighted average diluted shares outstanding are expected to be approximately 199 million. Considering these inputs, we are forecasting fourth quarter earnings per diluted share of $1.41-$1.61. Moving to full year guidance.

Based on our third quarter performance and outlook for the fourth quarter, we are increasing our full year net sales and holding our adjusted earnings per diluted share guidance. We now expect net sales to be down 3.4% to down 2.1%. We now expect full year adjusted gross profit rate to be approximately 42.8%. Adjusted SG&A rate is now expected to be approximately 29.3%. Adjusted net non-operating expense is now expected to be approximately $216 million. Adjusted tax rate is now expected to be approximately 26.3%, and weighted average diluted shares outstanding are now expected to be approximately 201 million. Considering these inputs, we continue to expect full year adjusted earnings per diluted share of $2.60-$2.80.

We are still on track to exceed our 2026 full year Fuel for Growth goal of $175 million by approximately $25 million. This progress has been critical to funding the Consumer First Formula investment and delivering bottom line results. Now for an update on capital allocation. We remain a strong cash flow generating business. Year-to-date capital expenditures totaled $155 million, and we now expect capital expenditures of approximately $215 million for fiscal 2026. We now expect free cash flow of approximately $600 million. This change relates primarily to the inventory investments previously mentioned. During the quarter, we returned $40 million to shareholders through dividends, and we repurchased 3.4 million of our shares for $60 million.

We also announced a new $500 million share repurchase program on top of the amount remaining under our prior share repurchase program. Our guidance assumes continued share buybacks through the end of the year. In Q3, we repurchased and extinguished 250 million notional of our outstanding notes originally due in June 2029. Our capital allocation priorities remain focused. We will continue to balance investments behind the Consumer First Formula, progress toward our leverage target, and the disciplined return of capital to shareholders while maintaining the financial flexibility to fund high return investments in the business.

In closing, our third quarter performance exceeded expectations and provided additional evidence that the actions we are taking through the Consumer First Formula are gaining traction. We remain focused on disciplined execution, investing behind the strategy, and creating long-term shareholder value. We believe these actions position us well to return Bath & Body Works to sustainable growth in 2027. With that, I'll turn the call over to the operator for questions.

Operator: Thank you. If you'd like to ask a question, please press star one on your telephone keypad. A confirmation tone will indicate your line is in the question queue. You may press star two if you'd like to remove your question from the queue. For participants using speaker equipment, it may be necessary to pick up your handset before pressing the star key. To allow for as many questions, we ask that you each keep to one question and one follow-up. Thank you. Our first question comes from the line of Lorraine Hutchinson with Bank of America. Please proceed with your question.

Lorraine Hutchinson: Thank you. Good morning. Daniel, now that you are one year into the Consumer First Formula, can you talk through what the biggest surprises have been, both positive and negative?

Daniel Heaf: Good morning. Good morning, Lorraine. I cannot believe it has just been a year. Let me start by saying and emphasizing that we are exactly where we expected to be at this point. As you said, we introduced the Consumer First Formula a year ago on this call, and I am really pleased with the progress that we are making and pleased that the evidence is showing in our financials. Yet, I will say what I always say, that we are far from satisfied because we know that the potential of this business and this brand is far greater, and everyone here at the company is focused on returning the business to sustainable growth.

On the positive side, we are seeing traction on several long-term growth drivers. Digital has delivered two consecutive quarters of growth, and underneath that growth, it is built on strong new consumer acquisition. It is the quality of the growth that makes me feel better. Sales in our new distribution channels, Amazon and Ulta, continue to accelerate. International is consistently delivering high single-digit growth. Most of all, our product innovation is really resonating with consumers and beating plan. These are all what I consider to be long-term growth drivers that we did not have a year ago. Our path to inflection in 2027 is about scaling what is now working.

On the other side, I would say we are all pretty clear-eyed about the further improvements that we have to make. Our store performance is not where we need it to be. We are starting to see improvement in store traffic and customership trends, but we have to do so much more to earn the visit. We have been testing solutions already, better inventory, bolder retail theater, new store experiences and fixtures. These are all part of the solutions that we will bring to the balance of the chain in 2027. In product, I would say, we are really pleased with the adoption of the innovation. The innovation engine is now humming, and we have much more to come.

But the seasonal performance has been uneven. We need both growing franchises that consumers come back to and differentiated seasonal collections that they look forward to, and we know how to do this. So, Lorraine, in summary, I would say I am increasingly confident in the strategy, clear about the work ahead. Over the last 12 months, we have put these really important long-term growth drivers in place, and we will scale them to make them count across more of the business in 2027.

Lorraine Hutchinson: Thank you. Tom, can you walk us through the assumptions behind the decline in fourth-quarter gross margin implied in the guidance?

Tom Javitch: Yeah, sure. If you think about gross margin, the majority of the decline is in merchandise margin, and that is reflecting pressures as it relates to new tariffs and worsening inflation on merchandise margin input costs. That is over 200 basis points of headwind in the quarter. What happened is all of the new tariffs hit us right as we were in peak production, so it was challenging in Q4 to mitigate those costs. As we step into the following year, we are going to work very, very hard to mitigate as much as possible. B&O is down slightly, and that is due to the sales deleverage. The absolute B&O dollars are only up modestly.

Operator: Thank you. Our next question comes from the line of Ike Boruchow with Wells Fargo. Please proceed with your question.

Ike Boruchow: Hey, good morning, guys. Tom, maybe for you, curious on the revenue guiding Q4 sales down negative 2% to negative 5.5%. Following Q3, which came down 1%, I just would have thought the benefits of the Consumer First Formula would actually be building as comparisons are easing. Tom Javitch: Yeah, sure, Ike. I would say our sales guide reflects a couple of factors. First, as mentioned in the prepared remarks, our wholesale businesses gave us about a 2-point lift in Q3, as shipments are their highest in those businesses ahead of holiday peak. We are only projecting that lift to be closer to about 40 basis points in Q4. Second, I would say the consumer environment was pressured toward the latter part of Q3. The high end of the guide really reflects North American retail sales about equal Q3-Q4. We know our category is highly giftable. We offer high emotional content and a strong value.

So we do feel good about our product, our marketing, our marketplace execution, and we are prepared to fight to win our share of wallet this holiday.

Ike Boruchow: Got it. Then a follow-up similar to Lorraine's on the gross margin for Q4. So I think you are saying slight B&O deleverage, about 200 basis points of tariff pressure. I assume the remainder is fuel and oil. Can you comment if that is true? Can you comment on what the fuel oil pressure was in the second quarter? Is this a headwind that we should essentially think that will carry into the first half of next year when we think about the gross margins and the puts and takes? Thanks.

Tom Javitch: Yeah, I would say in addition to tariffs and the impact of crude, there is a little bit of investment in transformation, but the majority of the deleverage is, I would say, in that tariff is well over a point and the crude is approximately a point. If you think about 2027 or forward, we are going to work very hard to mitigate these costs as much as possible, primarily through movement of production, supplier negotiations, and procurement activities across the impacted areas. So our goal, which we will talk more about in 90 days, would be to mitigate some of these.

Operator: Thank you. Our next question comes from the line of Matthew Boss with JPMorgan. Please proceed with your question.

Matthew Boss: Great, thanks. Daniel, as we assess health of the underlying brand, could you speak to promotional activity and the interplay that you see with traffic? We saw the promotional activity in the third quarter with the 50% off event. Can you restore and drive traffic without increasing promotional in depth?

Daniel Heaf: Yeah, thanks for the question, Matt. Let me start by just clarifying a few points that came up in our prepared remarks. The Q3 merch margin was above guidance, and our AUR was flat to LY, so we were not more promotional in the quarter we've just passed. As I've always said, value is an integral part of our brand, and we're really proud of that. To compete, we're constantly refreshing our promotional playbook, and the 50% off deal that we offered in October was something new. New and existing consumers really responded to what was a very simple and clear offer. Turning to the strategy, it's not simply to offer deeper and more frequent discounts.

The Consumer First Formula is designed to create stronger product desirability and greater pricing power through better formulas, fragrance, packaging, and storytelling. As you heard, we've launched this first wave of innovation in the third quarter. All these products beat our expectations, and they are trading at a premium AUR. We exclude the new product franchises from many of the sharp price point deals that we offer. As we move into 2027, as I said, our focus is about scaling what's working, scaling those franchises from about low single digit of shop to high single digit of shop. We expect that sort of price-protected innovation to be a bigger part of our business in 2027.

So, you can see that over time, with the right product innovation, a stronger brand, more fun retail experiences, we will reduce promotions while at the same time remaining famous for fragrance, quality, and exceptional value.

Matthew Boss: Great. Is it return to growth at some point next year, or are we thinking return to growth top line for the full fiscal year?

Daniel Heaf: We're going to provide much more detailed guidance in 90 days' time, but what gives me confidence is consumers are really responding to the changes that we're making. We're starting, as we see in this quarter, to see that evidence in our financial results. Digital has moved from decline to growth. New customer recruitment and reactivation trends are improving. Amazon and Ulta and international are expanding the company's reach and showing strong evidence of incrementality. New product franchises are resonating.

The goal, and really 2027, is about taking these initiatives that have worked at a relatively and deliberately controlled scale in 2026 and making them materially better across categories, channels, and markets to build the repeatable platforms that take us to sustainable growth.

Operator: Thank you. Our next question comes from the line of Simeon Siegel with Guggenheim Securities. Please proceed with your question.

Daniel Heaf: Hi, Simeon. Yeah, body care, as we said a year ago, is the most competitive category, and it's absolutely where consumer expectations have evolved the most. Frankly, a year ago, our products in body care just hadn't kept pace with consumer expectations. Consumers want both fragrance and performance, and the benefit and the quality need to be communicated clearly to the consumer. We're just one quarter in to launching the new products, and those products do meet the needs of the modern consumer. As I said in the prepared remarks, we are really pleased with the response to Essentials, Reserve, Fruit Fusion, and Everyday Luxuries.

Those are all part of our body care category, so we feel confident that we're moving in the right direction. We have more innovation coming, and we will scale those platforms as we go into 2027. We bought conservatively in the third quarter and in 2026 because I believe that careful franchise management, understanding what's working, and scaling is the right approach. Now, on the flip side, Simeon, I would say that the competition has intensified. Competitors are now expanding into the seasonal collections and fragrance-led storytelling, and in many ways, I would say, adopting the playbook that Bath & Body Works helped pioneer. We had some great seasonal storytelling and seasonal collections in the third quarter.

I would say Preserve, but also Nightmare Before Christmas. Halloween was a significant headwind. The lesson that we've learned, and we are constantly reevaluating the strategy and reevaluating our performance, is that we can't build new franchises at the expense of seasonal collections and seasonal innovations. We're on the right track. I expect body care to improve as we go through the balance of the year and into 2027, and I'm confident that we have the skills, the teams, and the capabilities in place to deliver.

Simeon Siegel: All right. That's really great. Thank you. It's nice to hear your excitement around the wholesale. You guys mentioned the change in expected contribution to sales in Q4 versus Q3. Tom Javitch: Yeah. Daniel could answer the-

Daniel Heaf: Look, we're taking a very deliberate approach to wholesale expansion, and we are constantly looking for evidence to reassure us that it is incremental. We do have proprietary data that allows us to see with confidence that the sales that we are making on Amazon in particular are truly significantly incremental to the brand. We see that in the data, but also we see that in the fact that Amazon is offered at a materially higher AUR to our own channels, which tells us that consumers are really in a different shopping mentality. It's much more about convenience, free same-day, next-day shipping with Amazon Prime. I suspect that's driving that reach.

We know from our category data that over the years, Bath & Body Works lost significant share by not being on Amazon in a competitive and brand-right way, and our data is starting to show that we are winning back share as a result of now competing on the platform. I would say we're not even a year into running this business, and we see things that we can improve, and the team brings things that they can improve every week. The level of the marketing investment, the way that we're driving out the gray market.

I would say every week, every month, we find a new thing to do that improves performance, and we will continue to do that as we go through the balance of the year and into next year.

Our store associates call it an extra sales associate.
